# CLAUDE.md — Architect MUD

## What This Is

Post-singularity browser MUD in the HellMOO tradition. Text-driven, real-time, brutal, and funny. Node.js server with raw WebSockets, vanilla JS frontends (one HTML/JS file per client plus a sibling `styles.css`), PostgreSQL via Neon. No build step, no ORM, no framework.

### THOMAS and Architect are two different things

**THOMAS** — **T**he **H**ypermedia **O**nline **M**ultiplayer **A**dventure **S**ystem — is the **client and engine**. **Architect (Architect MUD) is one game built on it.**

The "System" is load-bearing: THOMAS is a *platform*, not this game's front end. With modification, and with the dev panel as the surface you'd do it through, somebody could build a **different MUD** on it. Roughly: `client/`, `server/engine/`, the dev panel, the plugin loader and the CODEX pipeline are THOMAS; the world in Postgres and under `content/` — Coldwater, the Basin, the orders, the prose — is Architect.

This is the existing "engine vs. content are separate" rule below, with a name and a reason attached. **The rule has teeth because of it:** anything that hardcodes Coldwater-specific content into an engine or client file is Architect leaking into THOMAS, and each leak is a thing a second game would have to unpick.

### GLASS is the renderer inside THOMAS

**GLASS** — **G**eometry, **L**ights, **A**ircraft, **S**treets & **S**tructures — is the 3D
renderer in `client/game/js/panels/windshield.js`: CPU only, a plain 2D canvas, no WebGL, faces
queued and flushed back-to-front because there is no depth buffer, and geometry that records
itself as affine `[a·fh + b·h + c]` data through `SHAPE_SINK`. Its only two viewers are the
cockpit canopy and the truck windscreen, which is where the name comes from.

`tools/modelshop` is GLASS's model editor, the way `tools/studio` is the map editor.

⚠ **Grepping for THOMAS misleads.** The only place the string appears is the dev panel's boot splash (`client/devpanel/index.html`, `client/devpanel/js/bootstrap.js`), which reads as if it names the dev panel. It doesn't — it names the platform the splash is booting.

### SIREN is the synthesizer, ORACLE is the voice inside it

**SIREN** — **S**ources, **I**ndex, **R**esonance, **E**nvelopes & **N**oise — is the audio
synthesizer in `client/shared/audio-engine.js`: `buildLayer` and everything that feeds it. A cue is
an array of LAYERS, each one a carrier oscillator and/or noise with an ADSR, a biquad, optional
drive, and up to two FM operators in series. Around it sit the 32-voice pool with priority
stealing, the tracker music player, the sampler, the generated-IR reverb, and the four buses.
`Index` is the FM modulation index — the parameter the whole synth turns on.

**ORACLE** — **O**rthography, **R**esonance, **A**ccent, **C**adence, **L**exicon & **E**mphasis —
is the formant speech synth, in the same file. Not a sampler and not a wavetable: a glottal
`PeriodicWave` source through a four-formant bank with a nasal antiformant, a separate noise path
for fricatives and stop bursts, a 27k-word lexicon over letter-to-sound rules, RP/GA accents, and
a prosody layer that falls at a full stop and rises at a question mark. It reads broadcasts, the
Architect, the library, and Read Aloud.

⚠ **ORACLE lives inside SIREN's file and does not use SIREN's layer builder.** It shares `driveCurve`, the noise
buffer and the buses, and it builds its own node graph — it never calls `buildLayer`. So an FM
feature added to the synth does NOT reach the voice, and the reverse. That assumption cost real
time before it was written down.

So the names nest: **THOMAS** is the platform, **GLASS** draws it, **SIREN** sounds it, **ORACLE**
speaks it, and **Architect** is the game.

⚠ **Grepping for SIREN or ORACLE finds only the file headers** — worse for SIREN, which is
already a domain noun here (the emergency siren, the dive siren, the cockpit warnings: ~26
case-sensitive hits that have nothing to do with the synth). Like THOMAS, these name a system
rather than an identifier, and nothing in the code is called either. The synth's own vocabulary is
`buildLayer`, `layer.fm`, `voices`; the voice's is `Speech`, `pronounceWord`, `PH`, `voiceFromName`.
The dev panel's **Voice Lab** tab is ORACLE's tuning surface, the way the Modelshop is GLASS's.

- [docs/proposals/engine-plugin-boundary.md](docs/proposals/engine-plugin-boundary.md) — substrates/laws/registries vs. systems + the litmus tests. Read before deciding where new code lives
- [docs/audits/](docs/audits/README.md) — reusable prompts that challenge the design at its silent seams; start at the [index](docs/audits/README.md), the seminal one is [source-of-truth-audit.md](docs/audits/source-of-truth-audit.md)
- [docs/ops-usage-watch.md](docs/ops-usage-watch.md) — **the free-tier budget, as a daily measurement** (`npm run ops:usage`). Pulls real consumption from the Neon and Render APIs and grades it on **pace, not total** — "4.1 of 5 GB" arrives too late to act on, so cycle metrics are projected and the bands sit on the projection. Read before touching anything that costs egress. The rule the whole thing exists for: egress is `boot payload × world loads per day`, and in August 2026 **nothing was watching the first factor** while a doc header said "~4.7MB" months after it was ~30MB — so the report measures both factors from prod and prints their product beside Neon's own number, because **the divergence is the finding**: model ≈ API means boot is the budget and payload is the lever, model ≪ API means trimming the payload is wasted work. Four traps, each of which reads as a reassuring number rather than an error. ⚠ **Neon's `/consumption_history` is Scale-plan-only** and answers the *same* 403 for every metric name, so it looks like an auth failure — the free-accessible route is the project object, which carries the counters as plain fields plus the real `consumption_period_start`. ⚠ **Render's bandwidth series is in MEGABYTES** (each series declares its own `unit`), so summing it as bytes understates by 10⁶ and presents as a permanently empty bandwidth row. ⚠ **Render reports no instance-count for free services** — `200` with `[]`, a plan limitation and not a zero; **`/metrics/cpu` is the way in**, since it only emits while an instance is RUNNING, so its sample timeline is uptime and its gaps are spin-downs (⚠ measure HOURS over the billing cycle but the cold-start RATE over a trailing 7 days, or the rate is unusable for the first days of every month). That correction also killed the original cold-start source: **`player_count_log` undercounts by half** (13.4/day vs 6.8 measured) because it is written by `schedule('1m', …)` and **scheduler.js idle-gates every callback by default**, so its gaps mean "down OR up-but-empty" and an idle stretch either side of a restart merges into one — the low figure being the dangerous direction for a budget alarm. And ⚠ **attribution owns its own pool on `PROD_DATABASE_URL`**, because `db.js` reads `DATABASE_URL` — the *local* DB, whose `player_count_log` is empty, so the model would silently evaporate. Also: grading is against **FREE** ceilings even though Neon is currently on `launch_v3`, since "could we go back to free" is the actual question; limits carry a source URL and a `checkedOn` date and go stale at 90 days; and **the alarm never raises the alarm** — a webhook failure prints and returns false, exit code stays 0 even at CRITICAL, because a monitor that reddens a task daily is a monitor that gets muted (as built; the Actions cron is the one deferred piece)

**Content & authoring**

- [plugins/cooking/README.md](plugins/cooking/README.md) — **cooking, as built**, and the doc to read before touching a dish. Beyond the 47-recipe catalog it now holds three rules worth knowing before you change anything: **boiling** — dry starch cooks in liquid, not in heat, so `cook` refuses a dry pan of pasta and `fill <vessel>` at a `water_source` puts `item_water` in the pan as an ORDINARY INGREDIENT ROW rather than as a second fluid-scalar representation nothing else would have understood; the `cooking_medium` tag is what keeps that honest — **a liquid for every question about the PAN (the starch gate, `hadLiquid`, boil-vs-sizzle) and invisible to every question about the DISH** (no cook session so it can never burn, no signature, no bands, no name), because without that split "penne and two bottles of water" is a valid pan of sauce again with a tap to make it free; **improvised dishes** (`improvised.js`) mean an unmatched pan is named off a family table rather than becoming slop — *food makes a dish, non-food makes a mess* — capped at `superb` so **`masterful` stays the authored catalog's alone**; and **player recipes** (`recipes.js`) are identified by their SIGNATURE, never their name, which is why renaming is free, one combination can only be saved once, and seasoning isn't part of the identity. Shared as a tradeable card or by `recipe teach`, author travelling with it either way. Also the **shopping list** (`shoplist`), whose rule is that it **stores what you want, never what you have** — every tick is derived from inventory at read time, so nothing fires on acquisition and it can't go stale; entries are ingredient CLASSES, which is what lets the `shop.stock` hook mark whatever a vendor happens to stock that satisfies one. And **the burner is a dial** ([heat.js](plugins/cooking/heat.js)) — eleven positions with `low`/`mid`/`high` as NOTCHES on it rather than as the whole control, because a stove had eleven positions' worth of behaviour, three of them were reachable, the fine ones changed nothing at all, and the one thing nobody could say was **off**. ⚠ **The scoring axis is still the TIER** and not one profile was retuned: what the fine position buys is SPEED and PROSE, plus which tier you are in, so every position is load-bearing rather than three of them being. ⚠ **And the named tiers reproduce the old numbers exactly** — `LEVEL_SPEED` at each notch IS `STOVE_SPEED` for that tier and an untouched stove sits at the notch of its own ceiling, so every cook that has ever run behaves bit-for-bit as it did; regress asserts that identity, which is what stops the two tables drifting. The README's own note said a varying rate was impossible ("a piecewise clock to answer when is this done, and the whole architecture rests on `doneAt` being one stored timestamp") — the objection was right and the conclusion was wrong, because **a piecewise clock does not need integrating if you TRANSLATE the session instead**: `apparentNow` maps real time onto the session's clock (the identity at rate 1, which is why nothing stored had to be migrated), and a rate change shifts `startedAt`, `plainDoneAt` and every recorded `heats[].at` and `acts[].at` together, so every fraction is preserved and a turn made at 50% cannot silently become a turn at 70% because you turned the gas down afterwards. ⚠ **Convert exactly once** — `apparentNow` is not idempotent, so quality.js's entry points take REAL time and convert inside and everything else passes `Date.now()`. ⚠ **Rate 0 is a real state**: the clock freezes, no beat is armed, nothing burns, and the ring stays held because the pan is still on it — which is what finally makes "off the heat before the gin goes in" a sentence the sim can express, a gap the HUD's own source had a comment naming. ⚠ **The dial position lives in RAM**, because `furniture.flags` is a CONTENT column and a knob somebody left on 7 would be exported into the world; what matters durably is in the session's `heats` log on the food, and boot restore seeds the dial back from it so a paused pan comes back paused. The HUD draws it as an actual dial with the **comfy band** on it — `wantedTierAt`, the same curve `heatScore` grades the finished cook against, so there is one curve and one reader and the dial cannot promise a band the scorer disagrees with; it says a DIRECTION in words and never a number. Also **every fluid pours by the MEASURE, not just the oil** — `pour` was gated on `fat_or_oil`, so the other 22 liquids (broth, stock, cream, gin, vinegar, tomato paste) went into a pan whole or not at all, and the catalog had already flinched from it: penne alla gin asked for `liquid: [2,3]` until "penne, gin and two bottles of water" turned out to be a valid pan of sauce, and the fix was to STOP COUNTING LIQUIDS. ⚠ **This needed no new representation and must never get one** — a cooking "fluid" is an ordinary ingredient ROW and rows already carry `portion`, which `unitsOf` multiplies into the dish signature; there are already THREE fluid models in the game (fillable's `fluid_amount` scalar on 52 items, drinks' `drink` object on 13 that are also fillable and held apart by a hand-written invariant, and cooking's rows on 17 tag-disjoint cookware), and a fourth would have to be taught to `hadLiquid`, `deglaze`, fond suppression, the boil gate and every dish's `needs`. ⚠ **A modifier's measure is a DOSE and a weighed fluid's is GRAMS**, forced by `unitsOf` counting the two classes differently — a fifth of a 600g soup base and a fifth of a 200g tin of paste are 120g and 40g, so one word would mean three different amounts and a dish counting them would be counting nothing. ⚠ **Water is not poured by the measure and now BOILS DRY instead** ([boil.js](plugins/cooking/boil.js)) — a measure of a medium would be an amount no recipe could ask for, but the AMOUNT had no reader at all, and state with no reader is how a codebase ends up with two ideas of how full a pot is. A pot on a hard boil loses its water and what is in it then catches on dry metal, which is also what makes the new dial DANGEROUS rather than merely variable. ⚠ **No tick**: how much has gone is an integral over the burner log the session already keeps (`heats` is a list of [time, level] marks, which is exactly what an evaporation rate needs), so every answer is arithmetic over stamps written when the player acted, and a pan off the heat is a pan not boiling because `rate: 0` freezes `apparentNow`. ⚠ **It reads the fine LEVEL rather than the tier** — the one place in the system where that is right, because this is a physical rate and not a score. ⚠ **The number is set against the BURN clock, not against taste**: `autoPlate` ends every cook at `burnAt`, so a pot can only run dry if drying beats burning — 125g of penne on high burns at ~2.4 min and dries at 5, a 1kg stew on high burns at ~9 min and dries at 5, which is why pasta never boils dry and a forgotten stock pot does. Going dry costs a rung off the ceiling through the same mechanism mince uses, plus a burn window collapsed to `SCORCH_GRACE_MS`, and ⚠ **it can only ever pull the burn point IN**. ⚠ **The counterplay is the point** — the pan says so in the HUD and narrates once, and `fill` now works on a pan already on the heat (it refused before, harmlessly, back when a pot could not run dry); topping up restarts the clock and **never clears the scorch**, which is what makes the warning worth running back for rather than worth ignoring. ⚠ And **taking the water out says so too** (`dryVessel`, from `drain`/`empty`/pouring a pan out) and **staging inherits the pot's clock**, or a second ingredient dropped into a simmering pan would report a full pot. Also **`pour`/`empty` tip a pan out or decant it into another pan** (the solids stay), an ingredient can be lifted back OUT of a pan from the panel, and the HUD **mirrors the log into itself** — it is a screenful sitting on top of the place every reply arrives, so a MutationObserver on `#output` copies each line into a feed inside the panel. ⚠ That mirror observes the LOG rather than hooking the three append helpers, same seam Read Aloud uses and for the same reason; and it is `aria-hidden`, because `#output` is the one live region and a screen reader is already reading every one of those lines
- [docs/reference/plain-writing.md](docs/reference/plain-writing.md) — **the writing standard for everything we write** (docs, comments, commit messages, READMEs, guides): the `claudish-to-english` spec adopted as a house rule. Say it once, at the lowest accurate abstraction, and stop. Cut contrast frames, staged emphasis, redundant orientation and aphoristic closers; decode `load-bearing`/`X-gated`/`surface` back into the relationship they describe; never widen a claim while shortening it. **Contractions are the default** (added 2026-09-04) — write "don't", "it's", "you're", and expand only when the uncontracted form IS the emphasis, or when the voice is formal on purpose (the Architect, Ascendant wire copy). The corpus was written before that rule and was swept in one pass: `scripts/content/contractions-pass.mjs` for `content/`, `scripts/prose/contractions-code.mjs` for player-facing strings in code (comments deliberately untouched), `scripts/prose/contractions-html.mjs` for the guides. Four carve-outs keep it off the fiction — in-world prose answers to [story.md](docs/story.md) instead, em dashes stay an Ascendant voice tell, a closing joke is not an aphorism, and the "⚠ this exists because X broke" pattern keeps its reasons
- [docs/items.md](docs/items.md) — every `items` field, which JSON keys the engine actually reads, the working armor format. **A description is 300 characters or fewer**: it's read in a room list, on a shop shelf and in `examine`, so it earns its length in the smallest of those. `node scripts/prose/trim-descriptions.mjs` audits items + furniture and holds the rewrites; the only exemptions are the betatape cassettes (the length is ASCII label art) and the hero posters (the poster IS the content)
- [docs/tags.md](docs/tags.md) — the tag catalog/helpers/Tag→Action registry, and how to add a tag cleanly (property vs. behavior)
- [docs/reference/item-facets.md](docs/reference/item-facets.md) — **how a list of items sections itself**: shop shelf sections and container compartments are one question with one answer (`server/engine/classify.js`). The rule that shapes it: **categories are derived and the STOCK picks the axis, never the author** — a grocer sections by storage and a gunsmith by type with nothing configured, because an axis that doesn't partition the actual list scores zero and loses. Read the four scoring rejections before tuning it (each prevents a list that's worse than flat), the **nothing-is-ever-lost** contract, and `storage_tier` — the one authored tag, because nothing in a fillet's tags says it's sold frozen rather than fresh. A list that doesn't partition usefully **stays flat, and that's a success**
- [docs/flags-keys.md](docs/flags-keys.md) — `flags` keys across zones/NPCs/furniture and which plugin owns each; **owner `—` means nothing reads that key** (item tags live in `client/shared/tagCatalog.js`)
- [docs/vine.md](docs/vine.md) — the VINE graph editor: file roles, schemas (dialogue/script/AI/broadcast/quest), editor internals
- [docs/ai-behaviour.md](docs/ai-behaviour.md) — VINE behaviour trees for enemies/NPCs: node types, condition/action catalogue, blackboard, pathfinding
- [docs/npc-clothing.md](docs/npc-clothing.md) — the `CLOTHING` personality table, auto-injection at `apiCreateNpc`, the `flags.clothing_layers` model
- [docs/bsm-format.md](docs/bsm-format.md) — the `.bsm` broadcast-script spec as parsed by `compileBsm()`. Read before authoring or changing any `data/scripts/*.bsm`
- [docs/amp-format.md](docs/amp-format.md) — the `.amp` audio-asset spec (the devpanel Audio tab's export): instruments, songs, sfx, ambient, samples. The sibling of `bsm-format` for sound — read before authoring or changing an audio preset
- [docs/content-pipeline.md](docs/content-pipeline.md) — one JSON file per entity under `content/`, export/import/lint, the CI deploy. **Cutover done 2026-07-08** — git is the sole writer of prod content

**World & place**

- [docs/systems-world.md](docs/systems-world.md) — world state, movement, ambience, sound propagation, spawning, minimap, scheduler, tunables (as built)
- [docs/reference/land-taxonomy.md](docs/reference/land-taxonomy.md) — region vs district vs terrain vs biome, one SSOT each. **Read before touching anything "district"/"region"/"terrain"**
- [docs/systems-terrain.md](docs/systems-terrain.md) — `flags.terrain` ground-surface SSOT + the dev-panel Terrain Painter; drives minimap/tablet/pacing, **not passability, not flight** (as built)
- [docs/systems-overland-void-travel.md](docs/systems-overland-void-travel.md) — transient (non-DB) waste rooms off a region's rim: the `movement.edge` seam + `registerTransientZone`. Read before touching transient zones or the map rim (as built)
- [docs/reference/building-styles.md](docs/reference/building-styles.md) — **the art direction, by quarter**: what a building in the Glasshouse, the Marquee, downtown, the deco set, the Yards, the waterfront, Old Coldwater or the Reach is made of, how it signs itself and what colour it burns. The doc to read before authoring or repainting a building; [building-shapes.md](docs/reference/building-shapes.md) and the [Modelshop README](tools/modelshop/README.md) are the mechanics. ⚠ **Its headline finding is that the built city is ONE BIOME** — eight of `BIOME_MODERN`'s eighteen rows have zero tiles and `HANZI_BIOMES` only ever matches `citycore` — so painting a district in `content/` does NOT change how the renderer styles it, and the east–west modernity term is doing all of the separation (Aurelia 0.79 in the west, the Sump 0.42 in the east, Old Coldwater 0.00). Also the rule that cost two rebuilds: **a building gets its name ONCE**, and any second fitting takes the trade word through `$trade`. **§3 is the regions and the orders** — the Null's Deadwater, the Wildblood's Scarletwastes, Terminus, the Reach and the Exodus — each marked built or design, and each keyed on the `color`/`authority`/`path` already authored in `content/orgs/`. ⚠ Its finding there: **three regions are faced in flat colour**, `plain` being 9 of Deadwater's 15 palette keys, 19 of the Scarletwastes' 29 and **29 of Terminus's 38** — the family with no pattern at all, so those regions currently carry no material read whatever their prose says. The Reach is the counter-example and the proof the approach works (15 of 26 keys `timber`, and the one region outside Coldwater you can identify from the air with the signage off). ⚠ **ALL FIVE CANONICAL ORDERS ALREADY HAVE GROUND**, which is easy to get wrong because three of the regions are not named after the order that lives in them: Deadwater is the Null's, the Scarletwastes the Wildblood's, and **Terminus is the EXODUS's** — settlement, wall, sixteen interiors and a launch pad at the exact centre, shipped 2026-08-18. Writing this doc got that backwards once and called Terminus "the Ascendants at industrial scale", which it is not. ⚠ The Exodus brief is the one to read before inventing anything for them, because its first draft was wrong in the other direction too: it read deniability as INVISIBILITY — *"ordinary and very slightly wrong"* — when the rule forbids the game explaining the MECHANISM and says nothing about the building being seen, and an order whose whole promise is departure needs something to depart from. They are **a launch complex nobody will confirm is a launch complex**, and both halves of that already ship: the Ascension (*"the beginnings of something that has been the beginnings of something for a very long time"*, its lowest welds redone in a better hand than the top ones) and sixteen interiors that carry the whole austere register without a single symbol (identical blankets, a bell with no rope, forty feet of table and neither end is the head). ⚠ The Purifier is the hinge and also already exists — a machine at the threshold that strips every mutation and augment before you may enter, an **airlock** in everything but name. ⚠ No visible mechanism anywhere: their doors already look automatic and are being opened by the people walking through them, so a launch structure with no cables and nothing feeding it is the same joke at a hundred times the size. ⚠ And never write the line that points at the best joke in the region — the plant moved inside the wall, so **they power the thing they disapprove of and never once mention it**
- [docs/reference/world-rendering.md](docs/reference/world-rendering.md) — how a DB tile becomes a building out the cockpit; palettes, decoration helpers, the **three separate "tower" renderers**. Read before "improving a model". Also **the berth** — `flags.berth` on a water tile beside a quay, which brings a freighter up the fairway, lies her against the wall while the gantries load her, and sends her off again with a different hull next call. Four rules worth knowing. **A mark, never a `building_type`** — `camp`'s rule pointing the other way: a building tile joins the CFIT collision sweep and leaves the walk graph, so a berth authored as a building is a permanent ship-shaped hole in the basin you can fly into and cannot swim through, occupied or not. **ONE CLOCK WITH NO SEED IN IT** — an arm is handed its own tile and nothing else, so a crane cannot see the berth beside it and the only thing the two can share is the wall clock; seed it per tile and the gantry and the hull disagree about whether anything is alongside, which draws a crane working over open water while a ship sits doing nothing. ⚠ **The gate is `shippingOn()` and NEVER `motionOn()`**, which is the general lesson here rather than a detail of ships: that test ends in `ADORN_TIER >= ADORN_RICH` and `ADORN_TIER` is a property of the BUILDING being drawn — the model pass sets it per tile and leaves it wherever the last building put it — so anything that is not a building asking it is alive in a city, frozen over open water, and silent about why. ⚠ And **parked is a ship ALONGSIDE, not an empty berth**: every other cycle in the motion layer comes home to its own u = 0 and that is what makes `RENDER_TUNE.motion = 0` free, but u = 0 here is open water, so the obvious reading of "park it" DELETES the ship. `scripts/shapes/moving.mjs` gates all four (mutation-tested 5 of 5) and had to pin **both** clocks to do it — the goose flocks run on `Date.now()` by design — sweep the cycle through `RENDER_TUNE.shipForce` so the sea holds still, and record only FILLED paths, because the ambient birds are stroked and drift on a per-frame integrator no clock can pin. ⚠ **AND THE STOW IS A LIFT COUNT NOW, NOT A RAMP, WHICH TOOK THE CRANE'S OWN TIMER AWAY** (`BERTH_LIFTS`): `load` ran 0..1 over the working window and the hull drew `round(load × 72)` off it, so a box arrived on her deck about every second while the gantry was on a 44 s cycle of its own — one clock, and still nothing to do with each other, she filling like a progress bar and the crane miming over the top of it. The window divides into LIFTS, a lift is one box, and the gantry's `u` IS the fraction through a lift. Four rules there. **The latch and the release are the BERTH's, never two literals in the arm** — the crane stops drawing the box on its hook at `BERTH_SETDOWN` and the hull starts drawing it on her deck at `BERTH_SETDOWN`, and written out twice the container blinks out of the air; colour comes the same way, both asking `berthBoxColour(k, ship)`, so what swings out over the water is what lands with nothing passing between them. ⚠ **No two boxes in a row share a colour, and that is not decoration** — a plain hash gives a run of three about one lift in six, at which point you are watching a box go out and a box of the same colour appear, which is exactly what it looked like before any of it was true. ⚠ **Two gantries take ALTERNATE lifts** (`BERTH_SLOTS`), because a lift is one box and two cranes working every lift is two machines setting down together and one box arriving — a crane's slot is `(wx + wy) % 2` off its own TILE and never off its seed, adjacent tiles always landing in opposite slots where a seed hash gets it right about half the time, and its duty cycle now comes from the other one working rather than from an authored 0.42. ⚠ And **the trolley stops over the box's own ROW** (`berthRowOut`, a distance from the quay FACE, the one landmark both can name): it was one fixed spot on her beam while the hull laid the boxes across six rows, disagreeing by up to a third of a tile with neither being wrong about anything it could see. She arrives part-loaded, because eight lifts against a 72-cell deck could not fill her. Gated by a fifth check in the same file, mutation-tested 4 of 4 — and ⚠ **its crane is the DIFFERENCE BETWEEN TWO SCENES**, never a colour found somewhere in the frame, since her deck is stacked out of the same six colours and would answer yes to any of them
- [tools/modelshop/README.md](tools/modelshop/README.md) — **the Modelshop** (`npm run modelshop`, :5181): the editor for GLASS building models. Phase 1 (the inspector) is built — every model, at any scale, from the cockpit seat or the cab, with the shape cage, the nine segments the bake keeps, the constant-term warnings and the adornment cost. The rule that makes it worth trusting: **it imports the real renderer**, so there is no second preview renderer to disagree with the sim about wall texture, night lighting, face order or glow; the server is a static file server that owns no geometry, no palette and no camera. ⚠ **Use the Cab preset** — `ADORN_NEAR` detail exists for a driver at eye height 0 and a cockpit almost never sees it, so authoring only from a cockpit is how near-tier detail ships broken. **Authored models are also built** — one JSON file per model under `content/building_models/`, `npm run models:bake` into `client/shared/building-models.js`, and one `case 'authored'` arm. The rule that made it cheap: it plugs in at `modelFor`, so an authored model is an ordinary model record and CFIT collision, the truck probe, shadows, occlusion, LOD and the cold open all read it **with no change of their own** — the worked example entered the cold open's skyline bake with nobody wiring it up. An author writes tile units at a fixed basis and tags what each field scales with; the affine triple is derived. ⚠ The registry merge is `??=`, so a file can never silently replace one of the code arms. ⚠ And read the `NaN` note in its README before touching the adornment dispatch: those helpers all guard with `if (p.f <= 0.1) return`, and `NaN <= 0.1` is FALSE, so a mis-named argument paints every adornment at NaN — drawing nothing, throwing nothing, and reaching the committed bake that way. **The differ is built too** (`npm run models:diff`) — but ⚠ it compares drawing OPERATIONS, not pixels, and is deliberately not named pixdiff: a real pixel diff in node needs a native canvas dep and a build toolchain in CI, against a repo whose premise is no build step. A model is rendered against a recording ctx and the traces compared over 48 cameras. It OVER-reports (a different draw order is the same picture and a different trace), which is the safe direction for a gate and means a non-zero distance is a question, not a verdict — the Modelshop Difference panel settles it in real pixels, where all 173 models compare against themselves at 0.000%. A port declares `portedFrom`, which flips the merge so data overrides the arm, and **the arm is KEPT** in `LEGACY_MODELS` so the claim is re-checked every push and `RENDER_TUNE.legacyArms` reverts the whole city with one flag (it reaches CFIT, not just the picture). Determinism is now asserted for all 173 models, which is the precondition for any of it. **The editor is built too** — create, edit, save and delete an authored model in the browser, with the form GENERATED FROM THE SCHEMA (a field not in the schema cannot be typed in by accident, the property the Studio gets from the tag catalog). Three rules. The schema moved to `client/shared/building-model-schema.js` because **three processes read it** — the bake, `shapes:smoke` and the editor — and the editor compiles in the BROWSER with the same `compileModel()` the bake runs, so the preview is the building the bake would produce rather than an approximation. ⚠ **Every edit makes a NEW record, never a patched one**, because `shapeForModel` caches captured geometry in a WeakMap keyed on object IDENTITY — mutating in place leaves the cage, the roof readout and the scale strip showing the pre-edit shape, which looks exactly like the edit not working. And ⚠ **a save the build would reject is ROLLED BACK**: validation runs before the write, but a bind collision is a property of the COLLECTION and invisible to one document, so the file is written, the directory re-baked, and a failure undoes the write — leaving it on disk reads as honest and actually just breaks the repo with a tool whose whole promise is that it cannot. It now has a real 3-D viewport — a LOCKED SPHERICAL orbit (⚠ a vertical drag moves on a sphere about the subject rather than up a line; raising the eye while holding the distance is a crane, and it is what made the orbit look right on buildings and wrong on vehicles — plus two limits, since there is no pitch term to take up the slack: the camera never comes closer than three times the subject radius, or the near end of a three-tile rig projects several times the size of the far end, and the arc stops at 55°, because a true plan view is not something this renderer can draw), pan on shift+middle/right-drag, wheel zoom, click-to-select, a **floating tool palette** (icons with tooltips, dragged where you want it, closed and reopened with T — a modal picker is the wrong shape for the surface you pick a tool from while you work), **Fork to editable** on a hand-written arm (its captured MASS becomes an authored model you can then add shapes to — ⚠ a starting point and never a copy: no adornments but masts, no per-box texture seed, and ONE frozen entrance facing, so the fork takes its own id, binds to nothing, and deliberately does not claim `portedFrom`), and **move/scale/rotate** on <kbd>G</kbd>/<kbd>S</kbd>/<kbd>R</kbd> with Shift as the vertical modifier throughout (height can never be a free drag: there is no pitch term in this projection, so no depth cue to judge it against). ⚠ **`fh` and `h` are DERIVED, never set** — they were sliders, which let the preview show a footprint and storey stack the game never produces; `buildingScaleFor()` is the sim own formula and **floors** is the control, because floors is what the world authors. **Vehicles are in the tool too, edited as PARAMETERS** via `renderVehiclePreview` (`aircraftFaces` + `drawAircraftModel` — a genuinely second renderer): their meshes have no capture and no geometry to author — but they are GENERATED from a row of plain numbers, so the row is what is edited. Nine of the fourteen subjects have one (five fixed-wing classes and the four trucks); the Mayfly, the Cub, both helicopters and the wreck are meshes somebody drew rather than proportions somebody set, have no row, and must never be given one. The rows moved out of `aircraft3d.js` into `content/vehicle_models/*.json` + `npm run vehicles:bake`, which changed no geometry — all fourteen meshes are byte-identical to the ones that shipped before the move, and that is the check to re-run if it is refactored again. ⚠ A change must BUST THE MESH CACHE (`aircraftFaces` memoises on cls+detail+armed+variant and nothing in that key says which parameters built it, so the first build of a class would win for the session). ⚠ A fixed-wing file is the RESOLVED row rather than a patch over `FW_DEFAULT`, which is now only the starting point for a new class. And ⚠ the schema can only say a value is JSON — a string where a number belongs reaches the builder as NaN, paints nothing and throws nothing, so `shapes:smoke` builds every authored vehicle and fails on one non-finite vertex. ⚠ Its lamp alpha comes off `sun.night`, so **the sun argument is a SHAPE (`{elev, dir, night}`) not a direction** — a plausible `{x,y,z}` makes that alpha NaN and throws `addColorStop … could not be parsed as a color` in a browser while passing every headless gate, which is how it shipped broken; `vehicleRenderSmoke()` now fails on ANY NaN reaching the canvas. There is a **dev-panel sidebar link** beside Map Studio, sharing one launcher (`LOCAL_TOOLS`) rather than a copy. **Textures**: in GLASS the palette key IS the surface — `draw3DBoxAt` takes one surface argument and `wallTexMixed` derives BOTH colour and material generator from it (the `seed` beside it never reaches the texture), so there is no second texture layer to expose; the picker shows all 283 keys grouped by their **material** (17 families) off `wallPaletteInfo()`, with no second copy of the `*_WALL` sets. **Import/export** a single model file, import validated with the same `validateModel()` the server and bake run. **Undo** is whole-document snapshots (sixteen hand-written inverses is sixteen chances to be subtly wrong) and ⚠ **the snapshot pushed is the state BEFORE the edit** — a form field mutates then calls back, so snapshotting at the callback captures the very change you are undoing and Ctrl+Z appears to do nothing; a per-file BASELINE holds the last entry state and a push stores that before advancing it **Phase 5, the port, was attempted, MEASURED and abandoned on the evidence** (`npm run models:survey`) — the tooling ships, the ports do not, and the number is re-runnable in a second rather than an opinion. Of 172 arms, 129 are expressible as authored boxes+drums and **79 are BYTE-IDENTICAL to their arm at the capture conditions** — but **0 port faithfully**, because ⚠ **capture freezes ONE entrance facing and 79 of those 79 break when the facing changes**. The control settles what that means: the arms differ from the shipping `drawModelLOD` in all 258 non-canonical-facing cases, so **an arm is not a pure rotation of its own capture**. That is fine for an LOD that only takes over past `lodNear`; it is not fine for a port that replaces the arm at arm.length from a truck cab. Two things it did establish: **the affine basis is exactly right** (0 of 79 break when footprint or storey height changes — the hardest-looking part of the format is the part that works), and **the differ earned its keep** (129 auto-generated models would have looked plausible in the tool and shipped as quietly downgraded buildings; it refused all of them and named the axis). Porting would first need capture to record all four facings, adornments to survive SHAPE_SINK, and per-box seeds + drum style functions recorded — each design, not effort. **The authored format is for NEW buildings** — and ⚠ **bind by NAME, never by type**: `modelFor` prefers a building_name over a building_type and **408 of the 416 building tiles carry a name**, so a type bind reaches almost nothing. The worked example demonstrates this by being caught by it (`type:foundry`, whose three tiles are all named and all have arms), and `shapes:smoke` now prints unreached authored bindings on every push — a warning, since authoring a model before placing its tiles is a reasonable order to work in. The blunt corollary: **no building in Coldwater today would be improved by an authored model** without overriding a hand-made arm
- [tools/studio/README.md](tools/studio/README.md) — **the Studio** (`npm run studio`): the file-authoring map editor. Edits `content/` with no DB in the process, draws from the build's derive pass, generates its forms from the field catalog
- [docs/reference/building-shapes.md](docs/reference/building-shapes.md) — **building geometry as data**: the model arms record themselves (`SHAPE_SINK`) rather than being rewritten, so the flight sim's own shapes now drive distance LOD, occlusion culling, ground shadows, per-point CFIT collision and the cold open's skyline. Read before touching a building model, the CFIT sweep or the flythrough — and for the `hwRaw` pre-clamp rule, the solved-not-assumed `[a·fh + b·h + c]` basis, and the `yaw` trap. ⚠ **A box's half-width was capped and its POSITION was not**, which are different rules: an arm authors a canopy at `fh * 1.14` while `fh` itself is capped at 0.44, so 79 of the 173 arms put mass outside their own tile and the worst reached **1.1 tiles from its centre** — a slab over the middle of the road. `segFit` is now the one function that resolves a captured box into the footprint the renderer draws (there were EIGHT hand-written copies of `min(hwRaw, 0.44)` and the new clamp had to reach all of them), `RENDER_TUNE.tileFit` is the A/B and `scripts/shapes/tilefit.mjs` the gate. ⚠ It is the ENTRANCE side only (a mass offset backwards is over its own yard, not a road), it TRIMS the outer edge rather than sliding the box (sliding buries an awning in its own facade), it runs AFTER the shape capture returns (a clamp is piecewise linear and every captured field must be affine), and ⚠ **the exemption is by ARM, never by palette** — the Reach, the Thornwarren and Terminus keep their boardwalks, but `laundromat`, `citybathhouse`, `comicshop`, `bar` and `pawn` are Coldwater buildings wearing a frontier palette and a prefix test exempts all five (as built)
- [docs/proposals/coldwater-infill.md](docs/proposals/coldwater-infill.md) — **fourteen buildings the city has no analogue for, all built**, dropped into open tiles inside the existing grid: a cinema, a bookmaker, a taxidermist, a lending library, a pool hall, an amusement arcade, a fishmonger, a cobbler, a photographer, a locksmith, a tattooist, a vet, an off-licence and a one-room museum. 341 content files, 14 `drawTypeModel` arms and 14 map icons; the model count went 213 → 227. Read it before siting any new building, for one rule that cost a revert: **an open tile inside a finished block is usually another facade's street access rather than a gap.** A facade is a non-standable revolving door, so building on a tile REMOVES it from the walk graph — 921,912 is open grass with buildings on three sides and is Citadel Financial's only way out, and the replacement facade could not have handed it back. `node scripts/content/sitecheck.mjs` answers that and three more (no standable road neighbour, a cut vertex, something already pointing at the tile) by flood-filling with the candidate deleted: **81 of 438 open core tiles are legal.** Three more that are silent when wrong: ⚠ **`world_exit_zone` means two different things** — on the FACADE it is the STREET and on every INTERIOR room it is the FACADE, and the field name does not say which; **every exit needs a `content/connections/` row** or the `zone_edges` projection drops it and the door does not open; and **`marker` must be unique** (MARK-4). Also the roster's own selection test, which is worth reusing: **a new silhouette, not a recoloured shop box** — `drawTypeModel`'s `shop` arm is `default`, so a building whose model is the shop box in a new colour adds nothing to the skyline, and that test cut four otherwise-good candidates (a stationer, an optician, a tobacconist and a bottle shop are each one glazed shopfront under a fascia). ⚠ **Two of them make a PLACE rather than a list**, which is the siting lesson worth reusing: the pool hall and the arcade face each other across the south end of Meltwater Row with the cinema a tile north and the bookmaker a street away, so four buildings within sight of each other turn the bottom of the Marquee into the cheap end of the entertainment quarter. The same four scattered across four districts would have been four landmarks and no district. ⚠ And the one deliberate exception among the fourteen: **Stuff It's sign is PAINT** — `bakeSignText` with `dn: 0` rather than the `night ? 1 : 0` every other caller passes, so it measures **0 grads · 0 blurs** at full night and was the one shopfront in Coldwater that is genuinely dark after six. ⚠ **Second Helpings is the second, and it needed `solid` as well as `dn: 0`** — the default `put` in `bakeSignText` lays a coloured halo, a dark edge and then a WHITE CORE over it, which is a neon tube and comes out white whatever ink you hand it. `dn: 0` alone buys you unlit white lettering rather than paint
- [docs/proposals/old-coldwater.md](docs/proposals/old-coldwater.md) — **Old Coldwater, the slums in the south-east corner, as built**: 27 tiles in the angle where the south Curtain meets the east one, five enterable trades, two ruins and a tent camp. The sibling of the infill batch and its stated opposite: infill refused to lay a street, paint a district or grow the map, and this does all three. Three rules worth knowing. **The streets come first, because there were none** — `sitecheck.mjs` passes a tile only when it has a standable road neighbour to hang a door on, and Kessler Street reached six of the 27, so the ground had to be given lanes before anything could be built on it. **The tents are a `mark`, never a `building_type`** — a building tile leaves the walk graph and joins the CFIT collision sweep, so a tent city made of buildings is one you cannot walk into and can fly an aircraft into; `flags.camp` on ordinary walkable dirt is what `deriveSurfaceCell` turns into the canvas. **The ruins ARE buildings**, for the mirror reason. ⚠ And the one that nearly shipped broken: **925,917 is a storm drain into the Under** — `zone_under_925_917` exits `up` into it and it exits `down` — and a ruin was very nearly dropped on top of it. Nothing in the content pipeline checks that a building is not being placed on a z-layer seam; the only tell was a ruin script printing nine sealed neighbour exits where two ruins can account for eight. Also: the district adopts `content/districts/slum.json`, which had been authored with zero tiles and no claim on it since before the Under shipped
- [docs/proposals/halcyon-fields-infill.md](docs/proposals/halcyon-fields-infill.md) — **Halcyon Fields gets streets, as built**: five new roads, a garden square, 42 buildings and eight new GLASS silhouettes in the quarter the Glasshouse is building south into. It was 164 tiles of which 27 were buildings and a hundred were meadow; it is 69 buildings, 64 road and a 3-tile green now. Four rules worth knowing. **Only two tiles could carry a road into the big block, and that decided the whole plan** — its north edge is a solid boulevard frontage, its west edge is the Curtain, and five of the seven Kerbstone Row tiles facing it were already built on, so the mews goes up x893 and the one other gap stays a plot. **The variety is in the SILHOUETTES and the eight new types add NO NEW COLOURS**, which is the palette block's own rule quoted back at it: an estate one developer put up in one campaign looks like one thing, so what a new type may bring is a twist, a bulge, a step, a point, a courtyard, a lens, an arch or a building that is visibly unfinished. **Eleven of the 42 have no door** — the plots with cranes on them — carrying `is_building` without `facade`, which is the ruin pattern: mass out the canopy and a map code with no revolving door. ⚠ **THAT NUMBER WAS THIRTY AND THE ARGUMENT FOR IT WAS AN ARGUMENT ABOUT CRANES APPLIED TO FINISHED BUILDINGS.** batch8's own file calls nineteen of the thirty "clad, topped out and let" in the same breath as refusing them a lobby, and a finished building on a made-up street with no way into it is a facade in the theatrical sense — a player walking Kerbstone Row met six in a row. batch9 gave those nineteen a door each: two or three rooms, a light in every room, a junction box, and eight keepers, with **Chain Free the apartment building** (three vacant units at `rent_cost: 200` plus the letting agent's own, which is the intended shape rather than a collision since `npc_residences` tracks occupancy off `home_zone`). ⚠ **The entrance direction is not a free choice** — `flags.entrance` has been reaching `deriveSurfaceCell` since batch8, so it is the side the GLASS model has been drawing its hood on all along, and moving a door puts it on a blank elevation while leaving the drawn one on a wall that does not open. ⚠ **And three bugs in `lib.mjs` could only ever show on a SEALED plot**, each silent: the neighbour loop strips every door the author did not choose and grants the one they did, and it `continue`d on a tile with no existing link — so the STREET never got its exit back at the door and the facade was enterable only from inside (reported by nothing but `content:lint`, as *"zone_edges would invent an exit"*); a `utilityAnchor` in a room reached by `up` claims the `down` that is already the stair home, and the guard for that looked for a room hung BELOW the anchor and could not see the anchor's own PARENT; and **only the anchor gets a free light**, so moving `utilityAnchor` off the entry leaves the first room any player stands in with no fixture, which is a valid world that reads as open air indoors. All three are build failures now, and `batch8.mjs` refuses a plot that has since gained a `facade` rather than silently stripping it back off. ⚠ And **the prose had to follow the model rather than the other way round**: how far a shell's glass got is SEEDED (a fixed fraction made eleven identical machines over eleven identical towers, which reads as one building repeated rather than as a site), the crane comes down once the cladding nearly catches up, `tileSeed` is `(wx + 512) * 73 + (wy + 512) * 149` so both are computable per tile, and six of the eleven descriptions were rewritten to match what the arm draws — the alternative being to key an arm on a building's NAME, which is Architect leaking into THOMAS. Also holds the **restyle**: the quarter's thirteen remaining masonry-SHAPED arms went round, and ⚠ **the material pass had already happened and the comments were stale** (`ty_going` was in PLAIN_WALL under an arm still saying "blind stock brick"), so what was actually left was the geometry. Three of those are corrections rather than restyles — **a gasholder is a circle and the arm was drawing a box inside a box**, a fan cowl is a circle, a transformer tank is a cylinder — plus **four `blinkLight` calls with their arguments in the wrong order** (`(rgb, alpha, now, r)` against `(rgb, now, seed, alpha, r)`, so the blink phase was driven by the frame's alpha and the light ignored the distance fade). ⚠ **The fire station keeps its red** and that is not the restyle being selective: "glass and chrome" is about form and material, and the palette block already says why the one warm thing on that skyline may never go
- [docs/zone-redesign-2026-07.md](docs/zone-redesign-2026-07.md) — the record of the 2026-07-09 zone re-imagining: what changed, why, and what's left. Read when a zone field's *shape* looks odd — this is where the reasoning behind it was written down
- [docs/lore-wildblood.md](docs/lore-wildblood.md) — **the Wildblood, as canon.** Not a systems doc and nothing in it is built; it is what they believe and do, and prose that contradicts it is wrong rather than differently imagined. Read it before writing any mutant, any Long Watch line about mutants, or anything in the Under. The fact most often got wrong by accident: **the Under is theirs and was theirs first** — the obvious assumption is that mutants come up from the south, and a character who talks about them as arrivals is mistaken. Three more that change how they are written: ordinary flesh is **the First Shape**, an evolutionary mistake rather than something sacred, and the mutations appearing in the wastes are not diseases but **instructions**; the mutagen is called **blood**, every tribe brews its own and nobody outside knows what is in it; and **the initiate CHOOSES** — they say aloud what they want before drinking, then get it (a **Gift**), get something else (a **Lesson**), or die (a **Refusal**, where the body simply told the truth), and all three are good days, which is the thing that makes them incomprehensible rather than merely hostile. ⚠ Whether the flesh rites extend to enemies is **never settled and may never be settled** — the Watch calls it cannibalism, the Wildblood call it inheritance, and the game takes no side
- [docs/systems-wildlands.md](docs/systems-wildlands.md) — the Curtain and the Wildlands beyond the South Gate. **Compound status: the map + wall are BUILT content; the systems on top of them are still design.** Its Wildblood camp section is **superseded** — that camp was built as a town in the Scarletwastes instead (below)
- [docs/proposals/scarletwastes.md](docs/proposals/scarletwastes.md) — **The Scarletwastes, as built**: a fourth region (93×52, 4,836 tiles, uniformly `redrock` **on purpose**, so it can be hand-painted in the Studio later — don't scatter terrain into it in code), and **the Thornwarren**, the Wildblood's walled town. Three rules worth knowing before you touch any of it. **The terror is on the approach and the inside is domestic, and nothing ever remarks on the difference** — the trophy road is a performance whose tell is that it is *maintained*, and the gate masks are quilt-lined so they don't chafe on a long shift; no NPC ever argues the point and no quest may ever reward a player for working it out. **The wall is grown, not built** — thorn that closes when you cut it, the deliberate anti-Curtain, and technically `building_type` and nothing else: `bt` (what stops a truck) reads that alone, while `is_building`+`building_name` would put 62 tiles into the map-code namespace and collide with Terminus's wall, which is the exhaustion Terminus hit at *thirteen*. And **radiation is a gradient whose hole is the town** — it peaks at a pool deliberately placed OUTSIDE the wall, and drops inside it, because a town that irradiates its own children is not this town. Also holds the **region-local acid rain** seam (see weather, below)
- [docs/roadmap-world-expansion.md](docs/roadmap-world-expansion.md) — the road to the 100×100 Coldwater Basin. **The canvas is built; the open questions in it were never answered** — read before a large world-expansion push so they get decided rather than re-discovered
- [docs/devpanel-js.md](docs/devpanel-js.md) — what each script in `client/devpanel/js/` holds, and the load-order contract

**Systems (as built)**

- [docs/combat.md](docs/combat.md) — to-hit, body parts, typed soak, cooldowns, enemy AI, loot; the authoritative source on combat
- [docs/systems-survival.md](docs/systems-survival.md) — hunger/thirst, radiation, mutations, drugs, buffs, sleep, status-effect framework. Also **what a drug LOOKS like** (as built): the vocabulary is [client/shared/drug-fx.js](client/shared/drug-fx.js), read by the renderer, the server and the gates, and it is **THREE SURFACES because each does something the other two physically cannot** — `field` (the weather-FX canvas, strictly additive: it is an overlay and cannot distort what is under it), `screen` (CSS over the whole client, the only surface that can warp the TEXT and the UI) and `window` (the flight canvas, the only one that can bend the 3-D world). **The look is DERIVED from `flags.drug_family` and is a three-act arc rather than one slide**, riding the phase engine that already walks come-up → peak → comedown — so a psychedelic looks like a psychedelic with nothing authored, and there is no second clock; `effects.fx` overrides per row and `fx.none` switches it off (a cigarette, which `VISIBLE_BY_CLASS` already refuses to show a bystander). Intensity is `authored × phase scale × potency`, so **tolerance dulls the view as well as the high**. ⚠ **A `phantom` hallucination resolves to `null` and must** — the whole illusion is that there is no drug, and a screen announcing you are high deletes it; it is one `if` in a shared law, so both the regress suite and the smoke assert it rather than trusting it. ⚠ **ONE OWNER PER ELEMENT**, and that is a fix rather than a preference: `#main` was already contested by `.tripping`, `.unhinged` and `.insane`, a CSS animation beats an inline style and the last rule in the sheet wins outright, so any two live at once meant one silently did nothing — symptoms resolve to **amplitudes** (`--dfx-sway`, `--dfx-blur`, …) and the stylesheet carries **one rule per element** in which a symptom that is not live contributes a zero rather than a competing declaration, which is what makes drunk-and-tripping one animation. ⚠ **The client holds a source until told otherwise** (only the server knows when a phase ended, so there is no timeout behind it): expiry, overdose, death and logout all clear, and a missed clear is a player tripping over a sober room for the session. ⚠ **Two claims on one surface flatten the arc** — `trip_start` sends no `profile` for a drug the phase engine drives and the drunk meter sends none either, while sources otherwise COMPOSE (screen symptoms union, field and window take the strongest). ⚠ **NO STROBE at any dose** (photosensitivity), and `pulse` needed a **baseline under the beat** because a pure `sin³` drew literally zero paint between beats — the smoke caught that. ⚠ **A "dark shape at the edge of sight" is invisible on a dark theme**: `spiders` and `veins` both shipped a draft in near-black over a near-black background and drew nothing; both are the background's OPPOSITE now, off `bgIsLight()`. ⚠ **And a harness that does not clear between frames is not showing you the game** — `_test.runEffect` stacks 90 frames on one canvas unless `clearEachFrame` is passed, which turns a 0.07-alpha heat haze into solid white columns; the live loop clears every frame. Also: every authored message takes a **string OR a list** (`pickLine`), and **the stat effects describe themselves** ([server/engine/drug-feel.js](server/engine/drug-feel.js)) from the block being applied rather than from prose authored beside it — so all 39 rows got it at once, a retune re-describes itself, and **a stat a drug moves with no sentence for it is a build failure**
- [docs/systems-mutations.md](docs/systems-mutations.md) — **mutations, as built** (Phase 1: the radiation half). Three rules carry the whole thing. **Expression, not a boolean** — every carried mutation has a 1-100 strength rolled ONCE at grant and only ever reduced by treatment, and everything downstream (soak, acuity, who can see it, what a clinic charges) derives from that one number; radiation deliberately cannot roll above 84, because a legendary rad mutation would undercut the reason to find the Wildblood. **Nothing is baked** — contributions are derived at read time and netted by the readers that already net gear and status effects, because the old model added stat_modifiers straight into `players.stat_*` and reversed the arithmetic to remove them, which is unrecoverable the moment one can be *partly* treated; the identity at expression 100 is a correctness invariant (it is what makes the migration net-zero on existing characters) and regress asserts it. **An authored effect key that nothing reads is now a BUILD FAILURE** — `effects` was authored on every mutation and read by literally nothing for months, so the vocabulary is a registry and an unregistered key fails the suite. Also: concealment is generic (a coat hides a torso mutation because it *covers the torso*, never because anyone wrote the coat into the mutation), detection is PER OBSERVER through `acuitySync`, `visibly_mutated` is now a write-through cache and **no longer a one-way latch** (treatment restores your standing, which is most of what the fee buys), and mutation body parts carry **zero hit weight** so unmutated combat is provably unchanged. Read the `bodyReport` trap before touching the paper doll, and the **deploy-order note** before shipping (the unbake one-shot runs BEFORE the code, or every stat is double-counted). **Phase 2 (the Wildblood half) is also built**: 24 mutagen mutations, the flask, the Quickening arc, natural weapons, active organs and enforced clothing conflicts. Three rules there. **A natural weapon IS a `weaponStats` object** — the same plain object a pipe produces, handed to the same `playerAttackEnemy`, so soak/crits/injury/body-parts treat a clawed hand identically and there is no parallel combat path; it returns `null` for an unmutated body, and it is consulted only on the UNARMED branch (pick up a pipe and you use the pipe). **An organ you cannot choose to fire is just a damage number**, so `shock`/`screech`/`morph` are verbs routed through the new `applyStrikeToEnemy` rather than passive modifiers — never write `enemy.hp` from a plugin, that skips the part roll, typed soak, damage observers and loot-on-death. And **a garment that does not fit is refused, never destroyed** — the body is the wrong shape, the coat is fine, and the way out is the `accommodates` tag carrying SHAPES (`TAIL`/`WINGS`/`CARAPACE`…) never mutation ids. The mutagen gate is enforced at `MUTAGEN_CONSUME`, the only path that spends a flask, and its refusals deliberately never name rep or flags. **Phase 3** adds `GRANT_MUTATION` (the authored VINE action — and it **re-checks the gate**, because an authored door is still a door) and the mutagen supply, whose scarcity is worth understanding before you "fix" it: the flask IS on a vendor's shelf, but that shelf is `min_trust`-gated on a numeric flag **nothing raises except the Quickening arc**, so it does not exist for anyone who hasn't been through the Pool. **Flight is mobility, not gravity** — the obvious reading of wings is falling slower and there is no fall-damage system to ride, so instead it reaches four seams that already exist: the ONE named exemption to `engine:impassable-terrain` (that gate's own comment always anticipated it, and it must never become a *gear* exemption — nothing purchasable opens a cliff), dry water crossing on the same path a boat uses, a flee-contest bonus, and the `swoop` dive attack. **Wings help you LEAVE a fight, never survive one** — the bonus is on the flee roll only and regress asserts `defenseBonus` is untouched. **Every effect key now has a reader and regress asserts the unconsumed list is EMPTY**; adding one without a reader needs a comment saying why plus the cap raised in the same commit. **Phase 4 closes the design's own gaps.** **Mutating is an INJURY** (`onset.js`, fired off the `mutation.gained` event every grant path emits): real HP that must be mended, most of your stamina gone up front, genuine weakness while it runs, scaling with expression and worse for mutagen — and **floored at 1 HP so it can never kill you**. That cost is what makes the Wildblood giving you a safe room for the Quickening a mechanic rather than a line. ⚠ **A regress suite that grants a mutation on the SHARED fake player must restore hp/stamina/statuses afterwards**, or the sneak and weightbench suites go red for reasons that look nothing like mutations. Three medical tiers at three prices: **diagnose** (a NAME, deliberately mechanically inert — an unseen mutation reads as "Something unnamed" until a physician names it, because a diagnostic that also *helped* would make not-knowing a penalty rather than an information state), **suppress** (temporary, reaches every contribution through the one `effectiveExpression` chokepoint, and reports as *held down* never as cured), **treat** (permanent, expensive). The social ladder (`reactions.js`) is **socially hostile long before it is physically hostile** — there is no "mutant = attack" rule and regress asserts none of its lines is violence — and it **never fires in the Scarletwastes**, a hard region check, because a town that recoiled from its own people would break the one thing the region is for. `clone_inheritance` defaults to `all`, which is exactly the behaviour that already shipped
- [docs/systems-nullcraft.md](docs/systems-nullcraft.md) — **Nullcraft, the Null's discipline** (Phase 1 as built): the fourth faction answer, where the Ascendants buy a better machine and the Null make somebody else's stop working. The rule that shapes all of it: **it never invents a second copy of state that already exists** — a surprising amount of this was already shipping camera-scoped and scattered (`security_devices.status_flags` has held `{jammed, spoofed, hijacked_by, looping, blinded}` since forever, `getInterferenceZones` already jammed zones, `isWitnessed` already read it), so this is a PROMOTION of that model onto bionics/drones/locks, not a new one. Three more decisions carry it. **Trace is RAM-authoritative and decays at read, never on a tick** — the engine file owns no table, a logout drops everything, and only durable damage persists, through the CONTRIBUTOR's own writer. **Targets arrive by gather hook** (`tech.targets`, the `workspace.provider` seam), and `apply()` lives on the target, which is what keeps the substrate free of imports from augments and surveillance — the integration is ONE line in `deviceStatus()` and ONE filter in `getAugments()`, so every downstream reader inherits Null jamming without knowing it exists. **Security is derived, never authored twice** — `overclock_max` does double duty, since it is already the whole mechanical statement of the faction split, making licensed chrome the hard target with nothing new authored. Read the applicability table before adding an operation (you cannot jam a hydraulic ram; collapsing that makes six operations one verb with six skins), the **telemetry-is-not-vital** rule, and `VEIL_CAP` — ghosting must never make a player unarrestable. Architect's network ladder is **design only**
- [docs/systems-psionics.md](docs/systems-psionics.md) — **Psionics, the Exodus discipline** (Phase 1 as built: psychometry + telekinesis). The fifth faction answer, and the one whose promise was prose only until now — the ideology row already said they *"still a room and move what is in it without a touch"*. Three rules carry it. **Deniability is the progression** — codex XIV deliberately leaves psionics unconfirmed ("the Basin's favourite joke") and Terminus rules that stating a thing kills it, so below Seer **no output line may claim a mechanism** and at Seer the prose may be impossible; the player crossing that line IS the arc, and the law lives in ONE function (`voice()` in `prose.js`) with a `violatesLowRank()` regress seam, because a law spread across forty template strings is a wish. **The body pays** — the mind is doing something the body was not built to support, so strain is a ladder of nosebleed → blood from the ears and failing sight → **seizure** (`knockOut`, the cosh's own seam, so you are killable where you lie) → **real damage through `applyStrikeToPlayer`**, which makes the injury plugin's observers hang an actual wound off it rather than this file authoring one; nothing invents a punishment, and the backlash ladder IS the deniability ladder, which is why it broadcasts to the room. **Psychometry reads the world's own exhaust** — `residue.js` subscribes to events that ALREADY fire into a decaying per-zone ring buffer, no other system was modified to feed it, and an impression is fragmentary and **unattributed forever** (cameras answer WHO; this answers WHAT HAPPENED HERE and sends you to find out who, which is what stops it deleting SPECTER). Also: **you major in one art and minor in another** — `psi_rank` is depth and `psi_focus` is breadth, top abilities are `focusOnly` and unreachable off-major at ANY cost, so there is no build that both takes bodies and walks dreams; resistance is **derived** from Cool/Brains/mastery/Static Mind/coprocessor with deliberately no second skill; and `PSI_CAP` is `VEIL_CAP`'s sibling. Read the **verb-collision trap** before adding a verb (it cost three renames: `attune` and `pull` are engine builtins, `read` is a specializedAction in six plugins, and plugin commands silently beat both classes — checking `plugin.json` command arrays is NOT sufficient), and the rule that `reach` fires `fireSpecializedAction` so **telekinesis always delivers existing verbs rather than reimplementing them**. Telepathy, precognition, biokinesis, projection/**dreamwalking**, compulsion, the Gate Keeper's Purifier and the armour taboo are **design**
- [docs/systems-mastery.md](docs/systems-mastery.md) — **Mastery, the Long Watch discipline** (BUILT, all eight disciplines: the swing seam, the purity cap, the stain, Read + Exploit, Composure, stances, techniques, the read window on all three Display Mode rungs, and Senses + Mind). ⚠ **Blind Fighting is NOT a `visibility.perceive` contributor**, which is what its own design doc prescribed — that hook hands every handler the same original args and keeps the LAST answer, `plugins/flashlight` is already on it and sorts first, so a second opinion REPLACES a torch's boost and a carried light stops working for anybody who trained; returning `undefined` when you have no opinion does not help, because in a dark room this HAS one. It rides the swing seam instead, and `combat.js` now hands the outgoing ctx the PERCEIVED `darkness` penalty, so a contributor can only give back a share of what the dark took and contributes exactly 0 in a lit room BY ARITHMETIC rather than by a guard. **Fear Discipline resists what you WITNESSED, never what you did to yourself** — an allow-list that fails closed, because most sanity loss in the game is self-inflicted and softening all of it would be a flat passive on the resource with no other defence; ⚠ the list is short because the game is, so **a new horror source opts in by naming its reason in `FEAR_REASONS`**. The rule the system is built on: **a Long Watch veteran must not look supernatural on inspection**, so it grants no permanent passive at all. **Read is the mechanical identity** — every other build in the game gets WORSE over a long fight and this one gets better, keyed on `enemy.templateId` so four hundred dogs is one row, and learning from MISSES is why it rides the new sync `registerSwingContributor` seam rather than damage-events. **Rank is stored raw and the purity cap applies on READ** (chrome and mutation lower the ceiling, floored at 10 — chrome makes mastery impossible, not discipline), and **removing them leaves a decaying STAIN**, without which chrome would be rentable. Read the ⚠ on the social ladder before touching it: `regardOf`/`standingGreeting` exist to be SAID, never CHECKED, and nothing may gate on them.
- [docs/systems-psionics.md](docs/systems-psionics.md) — **Psionics, the Exodus discipline** (Phases 1–2: psychometry, telekinesis, aegis, ergokinesis and the initiation; telepathy, precognition, biokinesis, projection/dreamwalking and compulsion are design). The fifth faction answer, and the one whose whole design problem is that stating it ruins it: **below Seer no output line may claim a mechanism**, enforced in ONE function (`voice()` in `plugins/psionics/prose.js`) with a `CAUSAL_WORDS` ban that stops "you sense" as firmly as "telepathy", so Codex XIV stays a joke the player disproves by living it. The body pays for what the mind does (strain lands on tissue), and `isAwakened`/`psiRank` are sync flag reads, safe from hot paths.
- [docs/systems-weather-extreme.md](docs/systems-weather-extreme.md) — severity scalar, gear-gated-lethal channels, no indoor safe haven, ⚠ forecast band, named hero events — **all built, steps 1–7d, acid rain and the EMP/ion storm included** (as built)
- [docs/systems-economy.md](docs/systems-economy.md) — credits/banking, vendors, crafting, IP/stat-raising, housing
- [docs/systems-ideologies.md](docs/systems-ideologies.md) — the reworked factions: 4 orders on a stance axis + path, rep/stance/path actions, the `ideologies`/`rep` command, `org_relations`
- [docs/systems-unrest.md](docs/systems-unrest.md) — **Unrest, phases 1-3 as built**: the conflict between the orders made observable, where before this the ten authored hostile `org_relations` edges were read by exactly one thing — the CODEX tablet reader — and no NPC behaviour reacted to any of it. Four rules carry it. **There is no player-facing readout, ever** — no verb, no gauge, no number — because the moment there is one the sim becomes a dashboard to optimise and the flavour dies; the dev panel is the deliberate opposite and gets every scalar, so the line is the CLIENT BOUNDARY rather than the data, and "is it working" is a question you answer at `/dev`. **Signal before effect** — nothing stages in a cell that has not carried a perceivable signal FROM THE SAME ORDER inside the last six hours, which is the whole difference between a checkpoint reading as the reply to yesterday's graffiti and reading as spawn noise; it also means a cell whose mood belongs to the authority cannot host an insurgency incident until heat has actually said something there. **The cell is derived, never authored** — a 12×12 block of grid coordinates, about ten over the built city, because the first draft waited on `flags.district` and twelve of the twenty authored districts hold zero tiles; nothing downstream knows what a cell IS, so painting them later swaps one key function and changes no scalar, incident or regress case. And **persist the ledger, never the incidents** — a live staging holds instance ids that do not survive a restart, so the checkpoint step writes `flags.checkpoint_cfg` on the live RAM zone object and nowhere else, which makes it restart-safe by construction rather than by anybody remembering. Also worth knowing: **three scalars, not two** (⚠ `pressure` integrates grip over DAYS and raises heat's BASELINE rather than heat, and without it the fast pair converges — a quiet cell could never generate what would make it loud and dead cells would stay dead for ever), **the two voices contradict each other and nothing ever reconciles them** (the em dash is the Ascendant voice tell, so the wire takes them and the street never does, and the faction split is readable in the punctuation before a word of it is), **place is spoken as a bearing and never as a name**, and **danger is audible from the tile you are standing on** — the warning fires from inside the hostile and checkpoint steps rather than from an authored line, because an author can forget one. ⚠ Read the ESP note before touching `plugins/emergency` (it is a SINGLETON — one module-level boolean beside one zone set, so the second `activate()` silently joins the first and the first `deactivate()` ends both; exactly one incident may hold it and the second declines) and the `fireHook` note before touching the ambient hook (last non-undefined result wins and `unrest` sorts after `district-ambience`, so it abstains hard at baseline and declares `after` in its manifest). **Phase 3 makes ELIGIBILITY a registry** — phase 1 had one gate (band high enough + a signal from the same order) and it is correct for exactly the two orders that fight over ground, so the Null and the Wildblood arrive as authored `flags.driver` values in `roles.js` rather than as participants in the tick, which needed no change at all. **The Null read GRIP and never the band**, so their target is the street the authority has already FINISHED pacifying (the band is heat + grip/2, so a pacified block reads quiet however heavy the hand still on it, and phase 1 would refuse it for being quiet) — and rule 1 still holds pointed at somebody else, since the signal a vendetta answers is the AUTHORITY's: the Null have no street voice and asking for one would make them announce themselves first. **The Wildblood read only the clock** — no band, no signal, no grip, no heat, because the one thing an incursion must never be is a consequence of what the city has been doing; ⚠ the night's way in is DERIVED from the night rather than rolled (a clock-only gate makes every cell eligible at once, and a stored roll dies at a restart), and ⚠ a night SPANS MIDNIGHT so the small hours belong to the night before, or the target moves at 00:00 and half a raid lands in each end of town. ⚠ **The burst is heat and NEVER pressure**, the scalar that raises heat's own baseline over days, or the Wildblood become a permanent tenant of a city they do not want. And ⚠ **`writes: 'none'` is a TRUTHY STRING**, so the withdrawn Exodus sail through every filter that merely tests for a role at all. Phase 2's seam is built and the favour QUESTS are the one outstanding piece — content, not code — [proposals/unrest.md](docs/proposals/unrest.md)
- [docs/systems-corps.md](docs/systems-corps.md) — corp = faction + owner + treasury + members + territory; influence tug-of-war, five power levers (Phases 0–3 built; espionage + NPC corp AI still design; venture half in [proposals/corporate-assets.md](docs/proposals/corporate-assets.md))
- [docs/systems-flight.md](docs/systems-flight.md) — aircraft, airfields, hazards, contracts, air combat, hangars. **One model only:** the continuous client-sim/server-reconcile loop; the old banded model and its minigame decks are unreachable legacy code. Also **`registerCellOverlay`** — the seam that lets the canopy show ground the `zones` table does not place. The void highway is real ground a truck drives and a walker stands on, and a pilot used to cross the same journey over `kind: 'air'`; the overlay is handed to `mapWindow` as its existing **cell provider**, so both sides go through `deriveSurfaceCell` and the two pictures are **identical by construction** rather than by matching effort. ⚠ **It is a RENDER provider and must never become `surfaceAt`** — `regionGates` and `isMapRim` both find the edge of the world by testing that `surfaceAt` STOPS, so synthesised ground under the index deletes every road mouth, the whole rim and the void's only entrance in one move
- [docs/systems-helm.md](docs/systems-helm.md) — the Echelon helm chase view + deck-cam cinematic, `echelon_bridge`-gated verbs (tablet mount pending)
- [docs/systems-trucking.md](docs/systems-trucking.md) — **THE LONG HAUL: driving the void** (as built, every phase). The road between regions as something you drive rather than endure, and three rules that are each a decision NOT to build something. **The corridor synthesises ZONES, never render cells** — `corridorAt` returns the shape `surfaceAt` returns and hands it to the flight sim's `mapWindow` through its new cell-provider parameter, so road auto-tiling, biome, buildings and fog come from the one derivation that already exists (`snapshot.js` kept its own copy and drifted twice, silently losing 144 street tiles and then park features; both now share `deriveSurfaceCell`). **The drive IS the crossing** — `current_zone` stays the void room and a node boundary emits `zone.entered`, so voidwalking's encounters, traces, hard nodes and teardown are triggered here and implemented nowhere here, and a breakdown finishes on foot for free. **The edge of the road is a law, not a wall** — no ground collision at all; the verge is slow and past the half-width you are bogged, never blocked. **The road BENDS, and the bend is a heading rather than an icon** — the corridor was a polyline of axis-aligned legs that needed no steering at all; it is now a curve, and the three rules in §1b are each a way that goes wrong silently. **The minimum turn radius is a correctness invariant**: cells are classified by distance from the centreline out to `OFFROAD_R`, so a bend tighter than that folds the verge band through itself and the odometer jumps backwards through the fold. **The tarmac had to get wider** — a one-tile band is fine on an axis and comes apart on a diagonal, where tiles touch only at their corners and the highway renders as a dotted line of squares (regress flood-fills the paved set demanding one 8-connected piece). And **the leash is applied when a bend is CHOSEN, never while one is driven** — pulling the heading back toward south every tile is the obvious way to write it and means the *straights* are not straight either, so no bend registers as an event because everything is one.
