Read the **explicit-road-icon** rule before touching `corridorAt` (the auto-tiler ORs adjacent road cells, so an unauthored corridor paints a crossroads for the entire length of the highway — the icon is still authored, but only ever as the nearest axis, never as a bend piece; the real direction ships as `road_deg`/`road_t`/`road_w`), the **surface tuning invariant** (`thrustMax × drive > rollFric × drag`, or the verge becomes a wall wearing a penalty's clothes), and the note that **the client reports where it IS, not how far it has come** — the server derives the odometer from position against its own geometry, because a self-reported distance lets a client weave and get paid for the tarmac. The cab is `paintWindshield` with `height: 0`; the wheel is `helm-wheel.js` in a new `absolute` mode (a boat sets a course, a truck holds a line). The rest of the design shipped too: an eight-speed box with a diesel voice, trailer articulation on one free variable (so jackknife is emergent rather than coded), the depot as a building you walk into with a maintenance bench, and the scale house that weighs **weight, not contraband**. The design record is [proposals/the-long-haul.md](docs/proposals/the-long-haul.md). Later: **the junction is a fork you can SEE** — a route is a trunk plus ONE limb because that is all the odometer runs along, so `corridorAt` only ever asked that road what was at a tile and the highway visibly ENDED in open waste at the fork; a route now carries its siblings and falls through to them, with ⚠ `corridor_s`/`corridor_node` STRIPPED from a sibling cell (a cell handing out another limb's odometer is wrong in a way that looks right) and the siblings built without a plan of their own, which both terminates the recursion and leaves them signless. And **the road signs itself**, in MILES — one conversion (`TILES_PER_MILE`) shared by the board, the `route` verb and the GPS, because a board saying 240 while the dash says 720 means nobody can budget a tank. Three rules there: **a sign is one TILE snapped at build time** (a tolerance band, which is right for a shed, renders a post as four identical boards in a row); ⚠ **the junction board looks 210 tiles PAST the fork**, because at an ordinary look distance the limbs have separated by ten degrees and it points every one of them straight on; and **passing a board is a SWEPT range**, never "am I near one", since the cab reconciles 4×/sec and a text tick covers a slab of road. The board is a `mark`, not a `building_type` (a building enters the collision sweep, and a sign that stops a truck is not a sign), and its rows reach the LOG as prose too (as built)
- [docs/systems-broadcast.md](docs/systems-broadcast.md) — channels, playlists, VINE scripts, NPC hosts, camera feeds, **plus the five live-assembled show modes** and the **two-sport pipeline** (Deadball baseball + Cluster Puck hockey): the `@sport` registry, the `narrate` seam, one league per sport, and the rink sub-screen
- [docs/systems-surveillance.md](docs/systems-surveillance.md) — SPECTER player spy networks + the witnessed-crime wanted system
- [docs/systems-shopalarm.md](docs/systems-shopalarm.md) — **intruder alarms behind locked doors, as built**: breach a shopfront and a box inside starts counting. The thing to understand first is how much of it already shipped — `lock:shopshutter` and `lock:shoplock` have both been hackable since the 2026-09-19 fix to the hack path (which had named `lock:hololock` literally, so a shutter declared `canHack: true` for months and could never be hacked), and 66 shopfronts carry one of the two. What is new is what waits on the other side. Four rules worth knowing. **The window is the mechanic and it is ONE clock** — the alarm arms when the LOCK gives, while you are still on the pavement, so walking in, finding the panel and playing the board all spend the same clock and `hack alarm` opens a game whose length is WHAT IS LEFT rather than a per-game constant; a faster model does not make the puzzle harder so much as make you commit before you have looked around. ⚠ **The `1s` sweep is the only authority on whether it tripped** — a board reporting a win after it fired finds nothing pending and no-ops, because a client a second behind is an ordinary client and not a cheat — and ⚠ **running the clock out is NOT reported as a loss**, which would cost the player their deck for a race they never lost. **The plugin knows nothing about shops**: the gate is `hololock.breached` plus "is there an `alarm_panel` on the far side of that door", so an office or a lock-up is alarmable by authoring one furniture row — ⚠ and `zoneId` on that event is where the HACKER stands, which for a shopfront is the STREET, which is why the event now carries the `door` (the one additive engine change this needed). **The law is `CHARGE_CRIME` with a new `breaking_and_entering` key at 4★ and `witness: 'always'`, never `WANTED_RAISE`** — the raw raise skips the evidence clip, the siren and `dispatchPolice`, and the police arriving is the entire consequence; `always` is the mechanic rather than a convenience, because the ALARM is the witness (it dialled them itself), so a dark street and a jammed camera buy nothing. ⚠ **No police are dispatched by the plugin** — surveillance already does it at any star count, and a second would put two cars on one call. ⚠ And `flags.lawless` swallows the charge silently, which is correct and means an alarm in the Reach makes a noise and nothing else. Five models on ONE axis (90s/diff 3 down to 15s/diff 10, authored as `alarm_tier`), the window and the difficulty moving together so a tier is a rung rather than a trade-off, and the tier is a statement about what the premises have to LOSE rather than how central they are. Three Display Mode rungs over one loop (`alarmgame.js` plus two skins, the demolition rule), where the board is a terminal block and ⚠ **the answer is always deducible and never a guess** — what the clock takes away is not your knowledge but your time to LOOK. ⚠ **A line goes with the sound, always**: a timed feature whose only warning is a noise is INVISIBLE to a player with audio off rather than harder, so the chirp prints one when and only when sound is off (`esp.js`'s own rule), and ⚠ **no strobe at any rate** — `body.shop-alarm` breathes rather than flashing, and `prefers-reduced-motion` drops even that. ⚠ **The red is cleared on every way out and re-asserted by the server**, because there is no timeout behind that class and a missed clear is a red room for the rest of the session (the drug-FX "client holds a source until told otherwise" trap, one system over). The purchasable tier for player-owned storefronts is **designed, not built**
- [docs/systems-jail.md](docs/systems-jail.md) — downed-while-wanted → Holding, gear confiscation + evidence locker, hackable cell door; the `player.respawnZone` seam
- [docs/systems-scavenging.md](docs/systems-scavenging.md) — posture-based perpetual search, per-zone loot tables, the 2D8−2D8 check
- [docs/systems-fishing.md](docs/systems-fishing.md) — posture-based cast-and-wait + client reel overlay, Fishing skill, rod gate; reuses the scavenging table schema
- [docs/systems-strays.md](docs/systems-strays.md) — **Cathode, the stray cat with a bionic paw**, and the new `search` verb. Four rules. **Hiding is ABSENCE, not suppression** — while hidden she is in a real, exitless, off-map den zone, so `look`, `getZoneNpcs`, SIFT, `attack` and `pet` all agree for free; a `flags.hidden` honoured by the room filter would need honouring by six other readers, and the first one anybody forgot is an invisible cat you can still stab. **The den is also the fail-safe** — it is her `home_zone`, so the engine's own boot placement and its own respawn return her to hiding *without knowing the plugin exists*, making "hidden" the default state after any crash. **We RETUNE the engine's respawn timer, never replace it** — `combat.js` sets `_respawnAt = now + 60s` and the 24h hide is that same field with a bigger number, written as the first SYNCHRONOUS line of the kill handler; let one `await` creep above it and the grief window silently becomes sixty seconds, which nothing else in the codebase would notice (regress asserts >23h; the `stray_cat_hidden_until` world flag independently covers the restart case). And **the memory is deliberately split across two stores** — warmth lives in `player_npc_relations` for tiering and VINE conditions, but the KILL is its own player flag that never decays and outranks everything, because warmth is shared substrate that shopping and washing both nudge, and killing this animal must not be launderable by being pleasant for a fortnight. Petting pays sanity on a 6-hour cooldown and **still works inside it** (never punish a player for petting a cat, just don't pay twice). A killer meets an escalating **refusal ladder that is the animal's, not the game's** — hiss, then bite, then simply gone — because a cat that bit on the first reach would be vicious and one that never escalated would be scenery; there is deliberately no rung above leaving (she never turns hostile, never gets a revenge arc), the bite is floored at 1 HP so a `pet` can never route into the death path, and **no rung ever says why**. Also **calling her**: `Cathode!` / `Here Cathode!!` / `call cathode` / `summon cathode` / `pspsps`, anywhere in Coldwater. An INPUT MATCHER, not a command, because bare `call`/`summon` are gametable's poker verbs and **SIFT does not arbitrate verbs** (it resolves which TARGET you meant; its `context.verb` is only a replay key) — so every pattern but `pspsps` carries her name and poker keeps its verbs, with regress asserting bare `call` is NOT matched. **Spamming deliberately does not help**: flat chance, no streak or pity counter, 60s between attempts AND 45min after she actually comes, because 60s alone is ~60 rolls an hour and any workable chance makes that a reliable summon. Only the RELATIONSHIP moves the number (4%/9%/20%, never for a killer — who gets the ORDINARY miss line, since a bespoke one would tell them what they did). Read the **`zone.npcs` reconciler is a net, not a licence** trap before touching any movement (`reconcileNpcMembership` sweeps set drift every 30s but believes `npc.zone_id`, so `moveNpcToZone` is still the only funnel), and the tone rules before writing a line — nothing ever explains the paw, and the killing is not funny. The `search` half is `plugins/search/`: a `search.provider` gather hook, **which never pays out** and whose failures are indistinguishable from an empty room (as built)
- [docs/systems-fauna.md](docs/systems-fauna.md) — **the birds, as built**: six species over the Basin, the flock cycle, perching, the hawk’s hunt, the murmuration, the voices and the room prose. One rule holds it up — **a flock is arithmetic, not an entity**, a pure function of its anchor tile and the WALL clock, because the renderer and [describe.js](server/engine/commands/describe.js) both have to answer for the same birds with nothing crossing the wire. Four rules worth knowing before you touch any of it. **The place, not the paint** — `flags.terrain` says what ground is made of and nothing about who lives on it, and Coldwater’s streets are painted `redrock`, so `placeOf` turns building-neighbour counts into a PLACE and both surfaces read it; ⚠ the renderer chose anchors that way and then filtered them on the raw terrain, dropping every pigeon, gull and songbird on **699 of the ~1,600 standable city tiles** while the room said they were there, which is the same bug describe.js fixed on its own side months earlier. **A ledge is derived from mass that is already there** — the top of a captured `shapeForModel` segment minus whatever sits on it, which is the list CFIT collision and shadows already read, so a bird cannot stand where an aeroplane would fly and no content file was touched; a setback tower works for free (The Meridian offers 21 perches, the tallest in the city is The Spire at 17.6 tiles, 364 of 375 buildings have one), and it is the PERIMETER rather than the plane, because a bird stands on the edge of a roof and a scatter over the top face reads as birds standing about on it. **`perchedNow` is shared and the ledge is local**, [murmur.js](client/game/js/panels/murmur.js)’s own line: a flock that wants a ledge where there is nothing to stand on falls through to the ground, which is what makes the feature inert over a field or a bay. And **a hunter takes the highest ledge in reach** — an accipiter is a still-hunter, so a perched hawk’s stoop is scheduled inside the PERCH phase at 38% against the soaring 16%, which is what puts it on a tower over a street the pigeons are working. ⚠ Read the settle note before touching the cycle: the window read `U_GROUND`, which is the GOOSE’s ground share, and past it the take-off term clamps and pins a flock in the formation it landed in — **a pigeon for 50.2% of its time on the ground, a songbird 28.9%**, the goose correct at 0.7%. ⚠ And the species has to reach the DRAW: `pushFauna` took a literal `'goose'` for months, so every other bird in the world was built out of the goose mesh while the budget was charged per species and the prose named the right animal. Gates are `scripts/shapes/fauna.mjs` and `scripts/shapes/perch.mjs` (`npm run gl:perch`, mutation-tested against eight breakages, and it sweeps the REAL city because a ledge is a function of the model AND the tile). ⚠ **AND THE STARLING HAS A YEAR NOW, SWITCHED OFF (`BIRD_TUNE.season`, 0 is the module as it shipped).** A murmuration was unconditional and nothing said so: `flockSize` is a hash of the anchor tile, so every starling flock in the world was 450–1,700 birds in February and in July, at dawn and at noon. The real bird has two independent cycles — **the year** (roosts full November to January, territorial pairs April to July) and **the evening** (⚠ a murmuration is a PRE-ROOST DISPLAY and not a thing birds do all day, which is the half a player meets every day rather than for three months a year). ⚠ **THEY MULTIPLY AND ONE THING CHANGES** — the flock's SIZE — so the budget, the thinning, the perch shares, the prose and `MURMUR_AUDIO_MIN` all inherit it without knowing it exists, and **there is deliberately no second gate saying "this flock murmurates"**: the murmuration IS the size. Midwinter dusk 1,045 birds against a midsummer afternoon's 6–16. ⚠ **IT NEVER REACHES ZERO**, because at `timeScale` 1 a game year is a real year and an honest breeding season is a feature a player could keep the game for three months and never see. ⚠ **AND THE FLOOR IS A BAND RATHER THAN A NUMBER** — clamped to one value, **every starling party in the world was exactly six, everywhere, for four months**, which reads as a bug and is invisible to every other check because the magnitude, the direction and the floor are all correct while it happens. ⚠ **The gathering's peak RIDES `dayEnd`** rather than being a second copy of dusk, or it drifts out of the window `birdDaylight` draws the bird in. ⚠ **The curves only ever take away**, because the bird-strike radius in `plugins/flight/hazards.js` is derived from the declared `maxFlock`. ⚠ And **the client had no calendar at all** — `env.date` arrived, was formatted into a HUD string and discarded — so the day is kept and **PUSHED** to the renderer (windshield.js must not import environment.js back: the cold open paints it before there is a session and every shape gate loads it under a DOM stub) and deliberately **not threaded through the view**, which would be another eight `hour:`-shaped hand-offs across four view files plus every bench, the trap `gl:snow` exists for; `RENDER_TUNE.doyForce` pins it as `hourForce` pins the clock, and a missing day resolves to the unseasoned PEAK, the safe direction for a budget. `node scripts/shapes/birdseason.mjs`, mutation-tested 18 of 18 — ⚠ two of whose claims were testing themselves first (the unseasoned baseline has to be taken while the flag is still OFF, and a search for the push also finds the catch-up call inside the dynamic import), and one of whose mutants was testing nothing, having defaulted the missing season to midwinter dusk where the seasoned answer IS the unseasoned roll by design
- [docs/systems-swimming.md](docs/systems-swimming.md) — swimmable water, stamina-scaled strokes, drowning, diving on a breath timer, soak→hypothermia (boat boarding + underwater content pending)
- [docs/systems-mining.md](docs/systems-mining.md) — posture-based deposit-working, per-zone ore tables, tool gate
- [docs/systems-jobboard.md](docs/systems-jobboard.md) — rotating legal early-money gigs over the quests plugin, greeter gate
- [docs/systems-casino.md](docs/systems-casino.md) — The Lucky Bastard: the self-contained `slots` plugin + a poker table reusing `gametable`
- [docs/systems-chess.md](docs/systems-chess.md) — **chess, as built**: full legal rules (perft-verified) on a 2-seat table in the same `gametable` plugin, which is what forced the real split — `TableBase` holds the seat/spectator/host-NPC/persistence half that any table game needs, `GameTable` keeps every line of poker lifecycle, and `game_types` finally *decides* something instead of being stored and ignored. The board is an **isometric 3/4 neon set rendered server-side in CSS** (the pieces counter-rotate so they stand up off the tilted plane; the shadow deliberately doesn't), and selection is two-step and server-side so the client never computes legality. Read the **`move` fall-through rule** before touching that verb (`move north` must never be eaten), the **0x88 note** (index 0 is a8, so White advances toward *lower* indices), and the **`startFen` trap** in the repetition counter. Lives in Material Advantage, floor 18 of the Solenne (as built)
- [docs/systems-bounties.md](docs/systems-bounties.md) — **money on a head**: a player funds a contract on another player out of their own pocket, and the four decisions the whole thing rests on. **The head is a physical item, in the corpse** — so a bounty pays whoever walks up to a board holding it, not the killer, which is what buys the theft, the ambush at the board and the third party who was watching the fight for free out of the inventory system. **The sheet names nobody** (a bounty you have to sign is a declaration of war nobody would post) and the target pays to un-mask. **Escrow is the number printed on the sheet**, so a poster can never advertise more than the hunter is paid; a claim closes the row with a guarded `UPDATE` *before* a credit moves, which is what stops two heads at two boards paying the same escrow twice. Read the accessibility section before touching any surface: `posterLines()` is the record and reaches the log at **every** rung, the client overlay is a skin over the identical characters, and every line is padded to `WIDTH` so the frame survives a proportional typeface. Not the law — see [systems-surveillance.md](docs/systems-surveillance.md) for stars and police (as built)
- [docs/systems-atm.md](docs/systems-atm.md) — ATM furniture: networks, fee/limit/faction logic, hacking, replenish tick, power dependency
- [docs/systems-instruments.md](docs/systems-instruments.md) — **instruments you actually play**: furniture flagged `flags.instrument` is sat down at with `play` and then played key by key on the player's own keyboard, and the room hears it. Three rules shape it. **A note is not a command** — noodling is 8–12 inputs/sec, so notes ride a thin `instrument_note` ws route that only `emit`s (the `tv_watch` shape) rather than running dispatch and printing a log line each. **Your own note never waits for the server** — the panel sounds it in the same tick as the keydown and tells the server after, because a round trip between pressing a key and hearing it is the difference between an instrument and a website; which is also why the instrument voices are the one part of `procedural-sfx.js` with **no `vary()`**, since two ears must build the same performance. And **no skill roll, deliberately** — the skill is the player's own hands, so `play` is the only verb in the game with no check behind it. Voices are FM off the `fm.depthTo` sweep that already existed (a collapsing modulation index is what reads as *struck*), and velocity opens the index rather than just the gain. Read the **token-bucket** note before touching the limiter (a bucket, not an interval, or a four-note chord is thinned to one) and the **keyboard-ownership** note before touching focus. First instrument: the black upright in Bishop's Blend (as built)
- [docs/systems-procedural-audio.md](docs/systems-procedural-audio.md) — sound generated from action+material+intensity rather than per-thing assets: the shared generator, the **parameters+seed wire format**, the material/surface tables and where to tune them. Also the **dense tier** (`sfxDetail`: off/limited/full) — footsteps voiced by the ground you just stepped onto, doors and locks. Three rules. **The gate is a `tier: 'full'` stamp on the NEW cues, never a category on all of them**, which is what makes `limited` a *provable* no-op against everything that shipped before it. **Nothing is authored twice** — terrain, `door_type` and the `lock:<family>` tag were all already on the world, so only `flags.floor` was new (591 interiors, seeded then hand-corrected), because `resolveTerrain` returns null indoors by design. And **a sound you hear ten thousand times is a fatigue problem, not a volume one**: alternating feet (random jitter reads as noise; an alternating pair reads as *walking*), a cadence floor that DROPS rather than queues, and `wet` from the live precip so a step in rain moves into the bed's own spectrum instead of cutting through it — applied as the DELTA, or rain goes on softening a marsh that cannot get softer (as built)
- [docs/systems-senses.md](docs/systems-senses.md) — smell/listen/sight as an engine substrate: the `zone.smells`/`zone.sounds` gather hooks and their **in-memory-only contributor contract**, acuity from stat/status/gear, overload, and why touch was left out (as built)
- [docs/systems-durability.md](docs/systems-durability.md) — **gear wearing out and being repaired**: `player_inventory.condition` as the item's HP bar, capacity **derived from `value`** (zero per-item authoring), five bands where the top two are mechanically free. Wear accrues **on use, never on the clock**, in memory on the combat hot path (**`wear()` is sync by contract**), flushed coalesced. Zero condition DESTROYS the item, so the Failing band + a fatigue warning on examine are load-bearing. Each repair makes an item likelier to break outright; a masterful hand repair REINFORCES — tougher and fatigue forgiven, which a bench can never do. Repair is a shared `repair` specialized action: field (capped at Battered) vs bench (an NPC flagged `repairman`, priced off `relationHelp`) (as built)
- [docs/systems-ascension.md](docs/systems-ascension.md) — **The Turning: the Ascendant defection arc, as built.** The first arc where a player changes SIDE, and the first faction ladder that is climbed rather than talked up. Read it before touching any Ascendant content. The thing to understand first is how little of it is new: the hook had been standing for months wired to one exit — the Long Watch send you west, Maresh recruits you, Vess walks you round the Spire, and The First’s own closing line already said *"your account is already open"*, and **there was no account**. Four rules. **The turn is a FITTING, not a conversation** — the point of no return already existed (`chromed_ever` burns the flesh path on your first install), so the quest walks you to a door the engine already had rather than inventing a flag. **Favour is `player_ideology_rep` and every rung was already load-bearing** — Known opens the clinic (`MIN_INSTALL_TIER`), Trusted and Inner Circle open the hardware (`aug.rep_gate`) and now the dialogue too, through the `ideology_rep` condition shape, so **no mirrored flags** and the fiction cannot drift from the shop. **The Rite is a CLAIMED DEATH over an economy that already shipped** — cortical backup → `assurance` → `backup` → die at the Uplink → print, so `plugins/ascendant/rite.js` adds only the verb that kills you; ⚠ its **wanted refusal is load-bearing**, because `onRespawnZone` will not claim a death at 1★+ and an unrefused wanted player dies UNCLAIMED with their chrome corrupted, looking exactly like it worked. And **irreversibility needed nothing built** — `chromed_ever`, the −200 resting floor, and the fact that the only door back out is the Exodus’s Purifier, which takes everything. ⚠ A repeatable quest’s dialogue offer gates on `op:'neq', value:'active'`, never `op:'unset'` — a turned-in quest’s flag IS set, so the obvious condition hides a repeatable forever. The **Long Watch mirror is also built** (§8), and finding it turned up the bigger bug: `grep -rl mastery_instructor content/` returned NOTHING, so mastery shipped a `train` verb, a rep gate, a purity gate and a per-teacher ceiling with **no teacher anywhere in the world** — the order’s whole discipline was unreachable while documented as built. Three instructors now ladder it (Pike 35 / Quartermaster 65 / Teague 100), which is also the Watch’s answer to a shelf of chrome: you climb their standing to be TAUGHT, not to buy a body. Their rite needs **no verb at all** — standing a watch is the absence of action, so it is a 180s `visit` and the engine’s existing cancel-on-any-command rule IS the test. New `mastery` condition shape gates it; ⚠ it reads `effectiveRank`, never `storedRank`, or chrome would open a door the discipline is meant to hold shut. Wanted-star suppression stays deferred (as built)
- [docs/systems-augments.md](docs/systems-augments.md) — **chrome as a machine you own**, the machine-path mirror of mutations. An augment is now THREE things that used to be one — an authored catalog row, an **ordinary inventory item** while it is in your hands, and an installed record once it is in you — which is why nothing here re-implements condition, quality, trade or looting: reusing `durability.js` and `player_inventory` got all four free, and the only reason to build them again would be refusing to be an item first. Four rules worth knowing before you touch it. **The schema split IS the design** — every column runtime mutates lives on `player_augments`, every column an author writes lives on `augments`, so neither needs an `excludeColumns` and `content:lint` has nothing to trip on. **`calibration` defaults to 100 because that reproduces the authored value EXACTLY** — the migration invariant that made the move off baked `players.stat_*` (the drift-prone pattern `mutations.js` was rewritten away from) net-zero for every live character. **Heat is memory-only, decayed lazily off a timestamp, and cooling off on logout is correct** — its durable residue is `condition`; do not add a column. And **zero condition does NOT destroy installed chrome** (unlike `durability.js` rule 4) — it goes *dead* and waits for a surgeon, because a thing disintegrating inside a torso is neither narratable nor actionable. Surgery is a real roll by an NPC flagged `surgeon` (five bands, `botched` permanently caps calibration, `margin < −10` destroys the hardware and you still paid); tuning is a **scoring** board, not a boolean one, because a boolean would collapse calibration into a coin flip; overclock is one authored field (`overclock_max`) that is the entire mechanical statement of the licensed-vs-back-alley split. Read the **failure_messages** rule before adding an augment (an overclockable one with no authored voice FAILS the build — "Bionic malfunction." is what that guards against) and the **ordering trap** in death corruption before touching any death path (corruption must be skipped whenever the death was `claimed`, or it deletes the rows a cortical restore is about to write back). Power, the unlicensed Promethean path and the paperdoll UI are deferred by design (as built)
- [docs/systems-relationships.md](docs/systems-relationships.md) — **what an NPC remembers about you**: the `player_npc_relations` substrate (familiarity/warmth → a six-rung tier ladder), hydrated once at login and read from memory thereafter (**zero runtime queries — `getRelation` is sync by contract**), lazy time-decay with no tick. VINE-authorable via the `{ relation: 'known' }` condition shape + `RELATION_ADJUST`; **`text_by_relation` falls back to a node's ordinary text**, so an NPC with no authoring behaves exactly as before (as built)
- [docs/systems-hygiene.md](docs/systems-hygiene.md) — **filth on a body** as an engine substrate: the contaminant→smell table for anything already written by bodily/combat/mis, a runtime `_sweat` meter, and `hygiene_washed_at` grime. **Sync and query-free by contract** (`hygieneOf` is called from the smell pass and from reaction paths). The zone-stain half lives in `commands/world.js`; this is the body half, and `ejaculate` is `misOnly` so a non-opted nose never smells it (as built)
- [docs/systems-mis.md](docs/systems-mis.md) — **the opt-in mature layer**: the two-switch consent gate (server setting AND player flag — off means every verb answers `Unknown command`, so an unopted player never learns the surface exists), the `sexuality` table where **`None` is a real answer and unset never defaults to male**, horniness tiers → climax (+10 sanity, a genuine sanity valve) with 5-minute-idle decay flushed in one statement, and the 8s ongoing-act loop whose every beat re-validates — **leaving the room ends it**. NPC arousal is in-memory only and NPCs remember nothing (not wired to `player_npc_relations`) (as built)
- [docs/systems-cleaning.md](docs/systems-cleaning.md) — **filth on a floor**, the counterpart to hygiene's filth-on-a-body: zone stains now run on **two clocks**, where unowned space is swept nightly as before but a room a player OWNS keeps its mess for a full rent cycle — which is what makes a mop worth carrying. The `zone-filth.js` substrate is sync/query-free (stains are RAM-authoritative), ownership is **contributed by plugins, never assumed** (the engine must not import storefront), and the 7-day cadence is **stateless** — derived from the game date, so a restart can't reset everyone's mess (as built)
- [docs/systems-library.md](docs/systems-library.md) — **nine public-domain books as a readable system**: the tablet reader, RP narration with the spoken line highlighted, minimize-and-keep-reading, and a tap-to-gloss vocabulary layer over prose that is **never rewritten**. **Read the copyright rule before adding a title** — the bar is US public domain (which is why *1984* is absent until 2045). `books` is `readTier: cold` and never boot-loaded, which is the whole reason the feature is affordable; note the `chapters->($2::int)` cast, without which every chapter silently reads blank. Not to be confused with [systems-codex.md](docs/systems-codex.md) (as built)
- [docs/systems-drinks.md](docs/systems-drinks.md) — **mixology, drinkware and the dish cabinet**: a finished drink lives on the vessel's `custom_data`, never as a new item row, because the whole point of a mug is that you keep it. Recipes match on ingredient PROFILES (pours, never grams) exactly as `dishes.js` does, with two deliberate differences — `vessels` is a LIST, and `medium` profiles (water, ice) fill a glass without scoring it. **Alcohol is DERIVED from `abv` × pours and lands on the ordinary `drug_alcohol` path**, so a mixed drink and a bottled one get drunk identically, and a zero derivation applies no drug at all. Hot drinks are appliance-gated on `flags.brew_tier`; cooling is derived from `hot_at`, never ticked. Also: **the `fromNearby` seam** — one opt-in option on `resolveInventoryItem` that lets a kitchen hold its own pots (as built)
- [docs/systems-faction-arcs.md](docs/systems-faction-arcs.md) — **the 40-slot ladder every order's questline is built to**, and the doc to read before writing any faction mission. Three movements: **1–3 benign** (the order is measuring whether you turn up), **4–6 tests that do not look like tests** (the player should be able to finish one without realising they were weighed, and work it out afterwards), **7–9 work with a cost** — with the **crossover at slot 7**, where you are sent against a rival order and that order makes you a counter-offer on the spot, because a pitch is only interesting when you have something to lose by taking it. **10 is the rite**, which locks you in; 11–40 are five ranks of six. Gating is **one numeric flag per order** (`lw_arc`, `asc_arc`, …) holding the highest slot completed — slot *n* gates on `op:'gt', value:n-2` and `Number(undefined)` is `NaN`, so an unset arc fails every gate with no special case; two hundred bespoke flag names would be two hundred chances to gate on the wrong one. ⚠ **The forty slots are non-repeatable, always** — a repeatable slot turned in twice writes an older arc number over a newer one and walks the player backwards; a repeatable job is a FAVOUR, which is a parallel thing. Also holds the **renounce** spec (leaving strips what the order gave you and makes you a permanent traitor to it) — designed, **not built**
- [docs/systems-demolition.md](docs/systems-demolition.md) — **breaching charges**, and the rule the whole thing turns on: **a charge is not a weapon**. There is deliberately no verb that throws one at a person — you wire it to a THING, choose how long you have, and the interesting part is that you are standing next to it. Everything follows from that: the fuse is PUBLIC (a charge nobody can perceive is an assassination with extra steps, and it would leave `defuse` as a verb with no way to learn about its own target), anybody can defuse anybody's charge including their own, and the blast does not care whose it was. The plugin owns almost nothing — damage is `applyStrikeToPlayer` (⚠ writing `player.hp` by hand still *looks* right while silently skipping the part roll, typed soak and every injury observer), sound is `propagateSound`, the crime is the `CHARGE_CRIME` action dispatched BY NAME so demolition never imports surveillance, and quest progress is one `demolition.detonated` subscriber in the quests plugin that yields the `demolish` objective type — and its failure mirror — for free. **Both minigames run on all three Display Mode rungs, and the middle one is the same game rather than a described one**, true by construction because `demolitiongame.js` owns the loop and the two panels are skins over it. ⚠ Read the **arson-vs-witnessed trap** before authoring a quest around a detonation: the blast charges a crime every time, so an untargeted `witnessed` fail condition fails the quest the instant it succeeds. Also: the sweep deletes before it detonates (invert it and a charge goes off once a second forever), the chosen fuse is clamped server-side, and a fumbled arming wastes the charge rather than killing you (as built)
- [docs/systems-stealth.md](docs/systems-stealth.md) — **sneaking, and knocking people out**. The rule that shapes it: **combat is to the death and stays that way** — a random KO mid-fight would be invisible (auto-attack finishes an unconscious body a tick later) and would make every fight ambiguous, so a knockout is *always* something somebody chose. Stealth is **borrowed, not built** — the notice roll is assembled from `senses`, `getZoneVisibility`, `impairment` and `posture`, and is **per observer**, so one person can miss you while another doesn't. An out-cold body is **killable where it lies** (reusing the dreams mind/body split), which is why finishing one is charged as `execution` at 5★ while the cosh itself is a 2★ camera-only crime. Police at ≥4★ now **take you alive** rather than killing you — the one route that used to skip jail entirely (as built)
- [docs/systems-dreams.md](docs/systems-dreams.md) — **two systems that share the word**: the per-sleep **dreamscape** and the **drug dreamzones** the dissociatives open. Both are now the same thing — 3–4 transient rooms built from `dream_templates`, **instanced per player and dissolved when the cause ends** (the authored, permanent, *shared* dreamzones were retired at instancing; anything still describing them as shared is stale). The load-bearing split is `current_zone` = where your mind is vs `zone.players` = where your body is — the sleeping body stays in the room, lootable and killable, while the mind is elsewhere. Read the **wake-path table** before touching anything that ends sleep (a missed path leaks zones and strands the player), the **`DREAM_VERBS` allowlist** before widening what works in a dream (`drop` in a room about to be deleted orphans the item forever), and **`persistableZone`** before writing `players.current_zone` anywhere (as built)
- [docs/systems-display-mode.md](docs/systems-display-mode.md) — **the three-rung Display Mode ladder**: one ordered player preference (visual / textgames / log) covering every system that has both a graphical presentation and a written one. Two predicates that deliberately share no words — `prefersTextMinigames` (surfaces you ACT through) vs `prefersLoggedPanels` (surfaces you only READ) — picked by asking **"if I delete this surface, is the player stuck?"**. Read the **migration trap** before touching the stored values (the middle rung is `textgames`, never `text`, because the old flag used `text` to mean today's `log`), the **tri-state** rule (never-chosen ≠ visual, which is what keeps poker's `textTable` default alive), and the **tick rule** (latch at an entry moment, never call a predicate from a tick). Also holds the ARIA contract: `#output` is the ONE live region, the pane is aria-hidden at the bottom rung, and the room description reaches the log there — if a system's record doesn't reach the log, that rung isn't done for it Also **who has the keyboard** ([seat-keys.js](client/game/js/panels/seat-keys.js)): a 3-D seat takes it on mount and on any press inside its own pane, and the command bar takes it straight back — one implementation of something **three of the five seats had privately and two had not at all**, so free look and the wheelhouse simply never took the keyboard and clicking the picture did nothing about it. ⚠ **Every private copy was bound on the BUBBLE, which the free camera beats**: `bindFreeCamPointer` binds `pointerdown` on the CAPTURE phase of the glass and calls `stopImmediatePropagation`, so with the camera off its mount the press never reached the pane and clicking the picture stopped handing the keys back — in the one mode where the keyboard is the whole control scheme. ⚠ `seatHoldsKeyboard()` is DERIVED (a seat is mounted and the caret is not in something you type into), which is the same test every seat's own key handler already makes, so `input.js` can hang its auto-focus on it instead of on a hand-written list of `isXActive()` calls **that was already missing the helm**. ⚠ A range slider is not somewhere you type (the sim's ⚙ tune panel is two dozen of them) and a text field INSIDE a seat is still a text field. Gated by `seatkeys-smoke.mjs` in both lists, mutation-tested 10 of 10 ⚠ **AND A SEAT OPENING IS NEWS TO THE D-PAD**: the location d-pad arms a WASD walk mode that binds W/A/S/D on the window, and while a seat is up those letters are steering something — so `claimSeatKeyboard`/`endSeatKeyboard` fire one `seat:keyboard` event and main.js disarms the mode and disables the button on it, rather than main.js carrying a list of panels it would have to be told about. ⚠ Its keydown guard names `seatHoldsKeyboard()` for the same reason its auto-focus sibling does: the hand-written list there was **the flight sim, the hangar bay walk and the depot walk**, so the cab, the wheelhouse, the boat helm, the charter cabin and free look all had their W/A/S/D eaten and walked the player instead. The two walk views claim no seat keyboard of their own and are still asked for by name (as built)
- [docs/systems-accessibility.md](docs/systems-accessibility.md) — **the settings surface, and getting a command in**: `A11Y_OPTIONS` as one table two surfaces render (the tablet page AND the `accessibility` verb, which has no tablet gate because the light switch cannot be inside the dark room), plus **voice input** — a mic button that is **off by default**, so a player who never asks for a microphone is never asked for one. The feature is not the Web Speech API call, it's the normalizer: general ASR is trained on prose and this game's input is jargon, so "wield rusty pipe" comes back "field rusty pipe" and a bare `n` comes back "in". Three rules — **aggressive on one token, cautious on many** (that gate is what stops the layer mangling the sentences it exists to help with), **the vocabulary is live, not listed** (nouns come from the `data-cmd` attributes the room pane and smartbar already render, never a static list that would be a second copy of the world), and **never invent** (unmatched passes through so the server answers `Unknown command`, because a guess that lands on a real verb runs it). Read the `scoreNoun` direction note before flipping it, and the **guard** before touching auto-send: `drop`/`give`/`attack`/`buy` are never auto-sent whatever the mode says. Also: **one submit path** (`submitCommand` in input.js, the same one Enter calls) and **interim results never reach `#output`**. Plus **Read Aloud**, the log reader, whose default is the load-bearing decision: `#output` is a live region, so **a screen reader is already reading it** and on-by-default would speak every line twice in two voices — it observes the log rather than hooking the append helpers, caps its queue and drops the OLDEST (a voice minutes behind the game is describing a fight that already finished), never reads glyph art, and rides `channel: 'ui'` so muting the television doesn't stop the game being read to you. **Natural (the browser's own voice) is the recommended mode, not the in-world synth** — the formant voice fits the fiction and is harder work to listen to for an hour. Read the enunciation section before touching the synth's text pass: compounds, initialisms, `₵` and `Dr.` were each silently wrong (as built)
- [docs/systems-posture.md](docs/systems-posture.md) — the `player.posture`/`sittingOn` contract, HP regen, stand-up triggers (split engine+plugin)
- [docs/systems-macros.md](docs/systems-macros.md) — smartbar macros: `;`-chained scripts, `$values`, if/else, macro-calls-macro **with arguments and `return`**, per-macro key bindings, and the `player_macros` sync. Conditions and `set` values are now **expressions** ([expr.js](client/game/js/expr.js), pure and fully covered headlessly) — which replaced `CMP_RE`, one regex matching `<name> <op> <number>` and the source of *every* limit the old grammar had: no boolean operators at all (`if $hp_pct < 30 and has bandage` was unwritable) and a NUMBER on the right, so no trigger capture could ever be branched on because every capture is a string. ⚠ The four legacy prefix forms (`has field bandage`, `in <zone>`, …) are matched FIRST and kept, because their arguments are unquoted and may contain spaces; nothing already written changes meaning and the smoke asserts it. ⚠ A malformed expression is FALSE and **never throws** — this runs inside the log's append path and inside loops — and ⚠ the Check button validates with `isWellFormed` rather than "did parseCond return something", or a typo'd condition passes Check and then silently never fires that makes the bar **follow the account** rather than the browser (⚠ its arrival test is the STAMP, never `remote.length` — an empty list from a server that HAS a row is a deletion, and reading it as "nothing up there yet" resurrects every macro the player just removed, on every login, forever)
- [docs/systems-automation.md](docs/systems-automation.md) — **client automation, as built**: triggers, timers, input aliases and macro variables. The rule that shapes it: **none of the three has a runner of its own** — every one ends at `runMacro()`, which already owns the step budget, loop pacing, the abort flag and client-verb routing, because a second executor is a second place a runaway loop can live. Read the **three guards** before touching triggers (a trigger fires commands, commands produce lines, lines fire triggers — that is a loop with the server in the middle, and the first trigger anybody writes is on a combat message): re-entrancy, per-trigger cooldown, and a global budget that **disables rather than throttles**, since a throttled runaway still spams forever with nothing to explain it. The budget is a **sliding window, not a resetting counter** — the burst across the boundary is the case it exists for and the smoke asserts it by name. Also: a broken regex marks its row dead and **never throws** (this runs inside the log's append path, so an exception there kills the log on every line until a reload), captures are substituted into the script string **before** the runner rather than threaded through as a scope that an interleaved run could overwrite, aliases expand **one pass only**, history remembers what was **typed** not what it expanded to, and `stop` switches timers **off and persisted** — a `stop` that leaves the thing which restarts the automation running reads as broken. Four later additions each lean on a seam rather than adding a mechanism: **state triggers** (`on hp_pct < 30 = drink stim`) ride `updateVitals` — the single funnel every `player_update` goes through — and are ⚠ **edge-triggered, never level-triggered**, or the rule fires on every vitals update for as long as the condition holds, which in a fight means drinking your entire inventory; **channel matching** (`trigger @loot …`) is the local answer to what other clients call a COLOUR trigger, and colour triggers are deliberately **never coming** — this client has no ANSI and ~30 semantic classes, which is the thing colour is a lossy proxy for, so a worse duplicate would be the mistake; **`wait for <pattern>`** is the primitive that makes a script a program (⚠ always bounded, since a script parked forever is indistinguishable from a hung client and `stop` cannot reach a runner off a step boundary, and ⚠ `offerLine` walks a COPY because a woken waiter can register another synchronously); and a **one-shot timer retires itself from the STORE**, not just the schedule, or it returns on the next reload and fires half an hour later with nothing to explain it. Later still: **multi-line triggers** (`3ln`), **groups** (`#combat`, a switch over a SET — ⚠ a group switch DISABLES where `off <pattern>` DELETES, because deleting eleven rules you cannot see is not what "off" should mean), **one-shot triggers**, and **the rest of the client setup following the account** via `player_client_config` — one row per player per CONFIG KEY, with `player_macros` deliberately NOT migrated onto it (deployed, working, and moving it is churn against live rows for no visible behaviour). ⚠ The conflict rule lives in `configsync.js` ONCE and nothing outside it compares a stamp, because six stores each implementing last-writer-wins is six chances to get the empty-list case wrong — and the empty list from a server that HAS a row is a deletion, not an absence. Finally **output routing** (`route @say = Chat`) — a route is a trigger whose action is "put it over there", so it reuses that grammar exactly; ⚠ panes are DERIVED, never authored (a pane exists because a rule names it), the routed copy is written even when the line is GAGGED because "send chat to its own window and keep it out of here" is the commonest reason to want it, and closing a pane HIDES it rather than deleting the rule. The other half of "split windows" — a fixed input area — was never a gap here, because `#cmd-input` does not scroll. And **speedwalk** (`3n2e`), whose entire design is one guard: ⚠ the obvious "only direction letters" test MATCHES `use` (u, s and e are all directions, as are `sew`, `wed`, `dune`), so a speedwalk must contain at least one DIGIT — `3n` walks, `nnn` does not, and a verb people type constantly is left alone. Also holds three things that look like they belong elsewhere and don't. **`gag` is resolved at COMPILE, not while the script runs** — hiding a line must be decided before it is mounted and the runner is async, so a `gag` found after a `delay` would be hiding something read two seconds ago; the line is suppressed BEFORE mounting rather than removed after, which is what makes the find bar, the scrollback cap and Read Aloud agree without any of them knowing gagging exists, and `msg-system` is never gaggable because that class includes the message saying your triggers just got switched off. **Command stacking's trap is `say meet me at the bar; I'll be late`** — free-text verbs are never split and the list is imported from `client/shared/dictation.js` rather than restated, because two copies drift into eating somebody's chat message; `;;` is a LINE-level escape, not a per-separator one, since the runner re-splits and a smuggled escape silently would not work. And **`.savelog` reads a session buffer, never the DOM** — the scrollback cap trims as you play, so reading the document meant the file began wherever trimming had reached, which is exactly the part you saved it to read
- [docs/systems-codex.md](docs/systems-codex.md) — **the game's backstory as a system**: the 30-second cold open a first login plays *before* the prologue speaks (the prologue holds all arrival prose until the client echoes `introdone`), and the tablet **CODEX** app that holds the full text. CODEX is a shelf of *typed* sections — two lore volumes plus the former standalone Ideology app, now its `orders` section with an unchanged payload. Chapters unlock one player flag at a time; a sealed chapter's prose **never leaves the server**, and `CODEX_UNLOCK` is the intended authoring route (an NPC explains a thing, you get the chapter about it). Not to be confused with the CODEX deploy pipeline (as built)
- [docs/proposals/trading-cards.md](docs/proposals/trading-cards.md) — **trading cards, as built** (`plugins/cards/`): a frozen snapshot of somebody, in three subject types on one shell — a **player** who minted themselves at a terminal, plus the **NPC** and **enemy** cards struck from world content when a series opens. **Only players mint, and that asymmetry is the justification**: the system deliberately doesn't follow anybody around, so you hand it the moment. Read the **budgets-not-truncation** rule before touching any text region (clauses are omitted whole, never trimmed), and the **spoken condition** convention (a Battered coat is "gone thin at the shoulders"; the band name lives on the back). Packs come from powered `flags.vends_packs` machines with a rolled sleeve size and a no-all-commons guarantee. **Buying and opening are two acts** (§5b): the machine is an ATM-style terminal panel, ₵900 buys a `card_foil_sleeve` ITEM that holds no result, and the **roll happens at `openpack`, never at the vend** — which is what makes an unopened sleeve untradeable-with-known-contents and un-stale. The reveal is a fullscreen cinematic in `client/game/js/panels/cardpack.js` whose one `RARITY` table drives the whole ladder; read the **client decides nothing** rule before touching it (as built)
- [docs/proposals/preparation-workspace.md](docs/proposals/preparation-workspace.md) — **all six phases built**: a floating text HUD over the cooking simulation. A domain-agnostic `plugins/workspace/` fed by the `workspace.provider` gather-hook plus **two providers** — `kitchen` (`plugins/cooking/workspace.js`) and `chembench` (`plugins/synthesis/workspace.js`), the second added with **no change to the workspace plugin or its client panel**, which is the seam proved rather than asserted. The rule that shapes it: **the HUD holds no gameplay logic — every action is a verb string a player could have typed**, which is why the payload ships literal commands rather than opaque ids and a regress case sweeps every one against the live verb registries. Provider gates are **coarse on purpose** — the HUD proposes, the verb decides; re-deriving a verb's real preconditions in the provider is the duplicate implementation this whole layer exists to avoid. The **Recipe Assistant scores only recipes the player KNOWS** — listing the undiscovered half with exact shortfalls is the ingredient checklist the Cookbook app deliberately refuses to be, and it would kill discovery. Read the **reservation** section before adding any soft-lock: nothing in the game locks an inventory row (trade explicitly refuses escrow), so `prepare <recipe>` is a re-validating plan, not a claim — it walks ordinary commands, stops on the first failure, never answers a SIFT prompt, and **stops at a loaded vessel without cooking**, because heat is where the skill is

**Before touching any system, read the relevant doc section if there's one applicable to the request.**

**Before editing any player command, check [docs/plugins.md](docs/plugins.md) first** — a plugin may already own that verb, in which case the engine handler for it is dead code.

## Core Architectural Rules

- **Engine vs. content are separate.** The codebase is the engine. World content (zones, items, enemies, NPCs) lives in Postgres and is edited through the dev panel. Don't hardcode content into engine files.
- **No ORM.** All queries go through the single `query()` helper in `server/models/db.js`. Keep it that way.
- **No startup migrations. Schema and content are managed deliberately, never on boot.**
    - **Schema** is the single source of truth in `server/models/schema.js` (`SCHEMA_SQL`, idempotent DDL). `npm run db:schema` applies it to your **local** dev DB. Production gets it through the CODEX deploy (below), not a manual `db:schema` against prod.
    - **Content lives in git (CODEX pipeline).** World content is one JSON file per entity under `content/`, and a **fresh database is built with `npm run db:create-local` (empty DB) → `npm run content:import`** (which applies `SCHEMA_SQL`, then loads the content tree). There is no checked-in `seed.js`/`seed.sql` and no boot-time content rewriting (the old startup `migrate()` was removed precisely because it disrupted dev by mutating content on every restart). The dev-panel `.sql` export (`/dev` → Power Tools → _Database Backup_) + `npm run db:restore -- dump.sql` remains as a **self-contained-SQL escape hatch** (backups, recovery), not the primary seed path.
    - **CODEX is the deploy path (git as source of truth).** World content lives as one JSON file per entity under `content/`; a push to `main` is the deploy — CI applies the full `SCHEMA_SQL` ahead of the additive content, backs prod up first, and is regress-gated. So idempotent schema DDL (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, deferrable-constraint swaps) **and** new content both reach prod through a normal push. See [docs/content-pipeline.md](docs/content-pipeline.md) and the `codex` skill for the authoritative flow.
    - **To change the schema:** edit `SCHEMA_SQL` (idempotent), apply locally with `npm run db:schema`, and ship to prod via the CODEX deploy (push to `main`). Never an auto-run boot migration. **Reserve manual one-shot scripts for what the deploy genuinely cannot do**, which is narrower than it looks. **The deploy DOES rewrite existing rows**: for any table with non-PK columns the import is `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (pk) DO UPDATE SET <every file column>` ([import.mjs](scripts/content/import.mjs) — `DO NOTHING` is only the branch for PK-only tables). Because a whole column is replaced, **editing a content file is enough to REMOVE a JSONB key** — dropping `flags.utility_room` from `content/zones/*.json` cleared it from 79 prod tiles with no script at all. So a one-shot is only needed when the new value **cannot be derived from the files**: reading state the files don't carry, or writing a table the pipeline doesn't own. (Corrected 2026-08-02 — this paragraph previously claimed the deploy "can never touch existing rows", and that sent two unnecessary one-shots at prod.)
    - **Running a one-shot against prod:** `node --env-file=.env.prod scripts/<name>.mjs` (the git-ignored `.env.prod` holds the prod `DATABASE_URL`). `db.js` enables SSL by **host** — remote ⇒ TLS, localhost ⇒ none — so this needs no `NODE_ENV` juggling and any `query()`-based script works against prod this way. Omit the flag to go back to local.
    - The export deliberately excludes player/runtime rows (accounts, inventory, password hashes); it carries schema + world content only.
- **Plugins for extensibility.** New behavior hooks belong in `/plugins/`, not in engine files, unless they're genuinely core.
- **No new sparse columns on `players` (or `npcs`).** New per-player scalar state goes in `player_flags` or its own feature table — never another `players` column. Same for per-tick/derived state: check the persistence tiers in [docs/architecture.md](docs/architecture.md#persistence-tiers-when-to-write-the-db) before adding a runtime DB write.
- **Every `query()` is a remote round trip — decide the read tier before adding one.** Prod Postgres is remote; latency lives in round-trip _count_, not query cost. Check the [read tiers in docs/architecture.md](docs/architecture.md#read-tiers-where-data-lives-at-runtime) before a new feature reads the DB: hot paths (per-move/per-swing/per-tick) never add awaited queries — serve them from the live player object, the world Maps, or a cache tier; never query in a loop (`id = ANY($1)` / `GROUP BY`); `Promise.all` independent reads; coalesce same-row writes; idle-gate scheduled ticks on `hasActivePlayers()` and register them via scheduler.js. **A cache is only as safe as its write funnel — grep every writer before caching a table** (why `furniture` and `npcs` rows are deliberately uncached).
- **UTF-8, always.** Several files (especially `client/game/index.html`) use Unicode glyphs and box-drawing chars (`₵ ⚙ ⏻ ╱ █ ☢`). When editing, preserve UTF-8 without a BOM — never let a tool re-save as Windows-1252 or it double-encodes everything into `â•±â•²` mojibake. After editing such files, sanity-check that the glyphs are still intact.

## Regression Testing — run it, and recommend it

`npm run test:regress` ([tests/regress.js](tests/regress.js)) is the pre-deploy gate. It boots the
world + all plugins (no server), sweeps every plugin manifest against the live registries (declared
commands/hooks actually wired), then drives real commands end-to-end through `handleCommand` with a
fake player — dispatch order, posture, move gates — plus every plugin's own `regress.js` suite.

**Run it without being asked** after any of these, and say so in your summary:

- editing the dispatch pipeline (`commands/index.js`), plugin loader, or any engine registry/seam
  (actions, events, hooks, specialized actions, move gates, posture)
- adding/removing/renaming a plugin verb, or changing a `plugin.json`
- extracting or relocating a system (engine↔plugin moves)
- **before any deploy/push of server code** — recommend it even for changes that look unrelated

Never wire it into production boot (same principle as no startup migrations — boot stays deliberate).

**The pre-push hook runs the same `npm run test:regress` you would type** — corrected 2026-08-21.
It called `node tests/regress.js` directly until then, which meant the PUSH gate and the TEST gate
were two different gates that everyone described with one sentence ("regress runs"): the whole
`pretest:regress` chain — `parse-smoke`, `automation-smoke`, `content:lint`, `query-lint`,
`docs:lint` and every `scripts/shapes/*` smoke — sat **outside** the push. A client file that did not
parse, a doc link that did not resolve, or a building model that threw could all be pushed by
somebody who had never run the suite by hand, and the hook printed "regress green" on the way out.
Same shape as the `docs:lint` bug two paragraphs below, one layer up. **Add a check to
`pretest:regress` and the push gate picks it up for free.**

⚠ **A red from that gate is not necessarily a red test.** On 2026-08-21 it printed "Regression suite
FAILED" while the suite had passed 8349/8349 — the run had been killed a sixth of the way in, no
error text, no summary line (the Neon pool transient noted below). The suite prints a trailing
`N/N passed`; **if that line is absent the suite did not finish, which is a different problem from a
failing test.** Re-run `npm run test:regress` standalone before you go looking for what you broke.

⚠ **And never read the exit code through a pipe.** `npm run test:regress 2>&1 | tail -4` reports
**`tail`'s** exit status, not the suite's, so a run with real failures comes back 0 and any wrapper
watching the exit code calls it green. This is the same class of mistake as the missing summary line
above and it is easier to make, because piping to `tail` is the obvious way to avoid pasting two
thousand lines of output. Added 2026-08-25 after four runs were reported green on that basis and the
fifth turned out to have been carrying a real `tv-reactions` failure. **Redirect to a file and read
the trailing `N/N passed` line** — `npm run test:regress > /tmp/reg.txt 2>&1; echo $?` — or check for
a `— FAILURES (n) —` block, which is what a finished-but-red run prints.

⚠ **Never run two regress suites at once — the second one kills the first.** `pretest:regress` runs
`scripts/kill-orphans.js`, whose process filter includes `tests/regress.js` itself, so starting a
second run sweeps the first away mid-flight. It dies with no error and no summary line, which reads
exactly like the Neon transient above and is not it. Same day, same session: a run was cut off at
line 1,524 of what turned out to be a 9,584-line suite, purely because another had been started while
it was going. **A complete run is on the order of 9,500 lines and ends in `N/N passed`** — if the log
is a fraction of that, look for the second run before you look for a bug.

**When you add a plugin or a verb, add a `plugins/<name>/regress.js`** (default-export
`async ({ run, check, getPlayer }) => { … }`; see [plugin-standard.md](docs/plugin-standard.md)).
Test code lives with the plugin and never loads in production.

Caveats: it shares the Neon session pool (pool_size 15) — if it dies with `EMAXCONNSESSION`,
an orphaned local `node server/index.js` is holding pool connections. A `pretest:regress` hook runs
`scripts/kill-orphans.js` to sweep these automatically before every regress run (and `predev` does
the same before `npm run dev`); run it by hand any time with `npm run kill:orphans`. It's Windows-only,
scoped to this repo's own entrypoints (`server/index.js`, `tests/regress.js`, `sync-commits.js`,
`scripts/dev.mjs`, `tools/studio/serve.mjs`, `tools/modelshop/serve.mjs`), and
never runs in production (`npm start` has no pre-hook). If a sweep can't reach it, wait ~90 s. Player
stat columns are `stat_brawn`/`stat_reflexes`/… (not `brawn`).

`pretest:regress` also runs **`docs:lint`**, which is **five checks**, not one — and the hook now
invokes the npm script rather than one of its files, so there is a single definition of what doc
linting is. (It called `scripts/docs/lint.mjs` directly until 2026-08-14. The two read identically in
prose — "docs:lint runs" — so `docs:verbs` and `docs:links` were never gated at all, and a real gap
sat behind that wording for months. The fourth was added on 2026-08-21, and the fifth on 2026-09-19, both by the route this paragraph
already prescribed: add it to `docs:lint` and the gate picks it up.) The five: **`docs:links`**, which fails when a doc link does not resolve;
**`docs:verbs`**, which fails when a plugin verb is not named in [docs/plugins.md](docs/plugins.md)
(a verb a player can never type — a client handshake like `readresolve` — goes in `NOT_PLAYER_TYPED`
in [scripts/docs/verbs.mjs](scripts/docs/verbs.mjs) *with a reason*); and
[scripts/docs/lint.mjs](scripts/docs/lint.mjs) itself, which fails
when a doc's **status header contradicts its own body** — a header asserting nothing is built
("Not Yet Built", "Nothing here is implemented", "DESIGN ONLY") sitting above a body full of ✅/`*Built:*`
markers. This exists because on 2026-07-27 an audit reported five shipped systems as outstanding work,
every error traced to a stale status line; `systems-weather-extreme.md` was titled "Design — Not Yet Built"
above a roadmap complete through step 7d. **A compound status is fine and is NOT flagged** — "Phases 0–2
built; rest design" or "STATUS: BUILT (design intent below)" pass, because mixed status is the normal case
for a living system. When you finish a system, update the line at the TOP of its doc, not just the roadmap
at the bottom. And **`docs:readmes`** ([scripts/docs/readmes.mjs](scripts/docs/readmes.mjs)), which points
that same status rule at `plugins/*/README.md` and adds one that only a plugin folder can have: it fails
when a README files a verb under work-not-done while `plugin.json` **declares** it. That manifest is the
one thing in the folder that cannot lie — the loader registers exactly what it lists — so it is the fact
to check the prose against. It exists because voidwalking's README said it had "**no** loot, encounters,
parties, ghost-traces, or frontier map yet" for months, three lines above a manifest declaring `loot`,
`frontier`, `scrawl`, `camp`, `flag` and `ready`. ⚠ **It reads HEADINGS ONLY, and that cost a draft to
learn**: a first cut that also swept running prose for a "no … yet" sentence found the one real case and
three inventions across 89 READMEs, because English puts "not" and "later" in one sentence constantly and
means nothing by it. A heading is a deliberate act of filing something under future work; a sentence is not.

And **`docs:hooks`** ([scripts/docs/hooks.mjs](scripts/docs/hooks.mjs), added 2026-09-19), which fails
when a hook that is FIRED anywhere in `server/` or `plugins/` has no row in the hook reference in
[docs/server.md](docs/server.md). That page states the rule the whole reference rests on — *"a name in
neither table is not a hook"* — so an absent hook does not make the page thin, it makes it WRONG: it
actively tells you a live seam does not exist. A sweep on the day it landed found **29 of 59 missing**,
nineteen engine-fired and ten fired by one plugin for another, which the page had no section for at all.
Every one was written up in its own system doc, which is why no per-commit habit caught it — the commit
that adds a hook writes the system doc and thinks the job is done. ⚠ **It scans three shapes, not two**:
`gatherHookSync` is a real third caller, and a first cut looking only for `fireHook`/`gatherHook` reported
`wall.tags` as a stale manifest entry rather than as an undocumented hook — a scanner blind to a calling
convention invents the wrong finding rather than none. ⚠ **And it matches a TABLE ROW, never the file** —
mutation-tested: with a plain `includes`, deleting the `shop.stock`, `wall.tags` and `sense.acuity` rows
left it green, because this page names hooks in its own prose a few paragraphs above the tables.

`pretest:regress` also runs **`content:lint`** (and a `precontent:import` hook runs it before any
`npm run content:import`), so a hand-authored content file carrying a runtime column — an
`excludeColumns` key like `zones.stains` — fails locally instead of surviving to the CI deploy gate.
Both mirror the CI order (lint → import → regress). `content:export` already strips those columns, so
only hand-written files can trip this.

`pretest:regress` also runs **`shapes:smoke`** ([scripts/shapes/smoke.mjs](scripts/shapes/smoke.mjs)),
which executes all 234 flight-sim building models in `drawTypeModel` (`client/game/js/panels/windshield.js`)
against a DOM stub — night and day, both entrance facings — and fails if any of them throws. This is
the ONLY automated coverage the windshield has. It exists because the only thing that ever ran a
building model was a player flying past that particular building: the Battery Acid Coffee Co. roaster
passed a palette KEY where `drawFacetDrum` wants a style FUNCTION, and it froze the whole sim mid-frame
the first time that cafe came into view. It also gates the shape-capture data (geometry must stay
affine in the footprint/height arguments). **Run it after touching any building model or mass
primitive**; it needs no browser, DB or network and takes about a second. If it fails on a browser API
rather than a real bug, add that API to [scripts/shapes/dom-stub.mjs](scripts/shapes/dom-stub.mjs).
It proves models RUN, not that they look right — there is no pixel comparison.

⚠ **AND `pretest:regress` DOES NOT RUN `npm run shapes:smoke` — IT LISTS THE SAME FILES ITSELF, AND THE TWO LISTS ARE NOT THE SAME LIST.** The sentence above is how everybody reads it and it is wrong in the direction that costs a gate: the hook spells out `node scripts/shapes/smoke.mjs && node scripts/shapes/freecam.mjs && …`, so a check added to the `shapes:smoke` npm script is run by anybody who types `npm run shapes:smoke` and by NOBODY on the way to a push. `glresidue` was added that way and sat outside the push gate until the regress log was read line by line and the script was not in it. This is the THIRD instance of this exact mistake recorded in this file — the pre-push hook calling `tests/regress.js` instead of `npm run test:regress`, and the hook calling `scripts/docs/lint.mjs` instead of `npm run docs:lint` — and the shape is always the same: prose says "X runs", two different things are called X, and the weaker one is the one wired up. **Add a new check to the `pretest:regress` CHAIN ITSELF**, and put it in the matching npm script as well so a person can run it by hand.

⚠ **AND A 2026-09-19 SWEEP OF BOTH LISTS FOUND THE FOURTH INSTANCE, PLUS THE DRIFT RUNNING THE OTHER WAY.** The direction this paragraph warns about is now clean — nothing in `shapes:smoke` is missing from the push chain. Two other things are true instead. **`gl:neighbour` is in NEITHER list**: [scripts/shapes/glneighbour.mjs](scripts/shapes/glneighbour.mjs) has an npm name, exits 1 on failure, and is run by no chain at all — and it is **RED right now**, naming 4 real defects (330 stroke points drawing through the building NEXT DOOR by up to **9.46 tiles**, worst `type:asc_spire`, `named:thelayover`, `named:buzzardfield`; plus `decal:gantry`, `decal:blade` and `decal:cranerail`). So this one is not a forgotten gate, it is an unlanded one: somebody wrote it, it found bugs, and wiring it would have reddened the push on day one. ⚠ **Nothing anywhere said so** — its header does not carry the "IT IS A REPORT, NOT A GATE" stamp that `models:quality` and `signhide` both do, so it reads as a gate that passes. Either fix the four and wire it, or stamp it a report; leaving it silently unrun is the one option that teaches nobody anything. And **16 gates run in the push chain but NOT in `npm run shapes:smoke`** — `framecost`, `glparity`, `atlasfit`, `glmesh`, `signtext`, `signfit`, `signhand`, `signfloor`, `signrange`, `glao`, `glfade`, `glsl-smoke`, `floorfallback`, `chess3d-smoke`, `rink-smoke`, `textui-smoke` — which is the second half of this paragraph’s own prescription going unkept: somebody who touches a building model and runs `npm run shapes:smoke` by hand gets a **false green**, because `gl:mesh` and the whole signage set are not in it.


`pretest:regress` also runs **`client:smoke`** ([scripts/client/parse-smoke.mjs](scripts/client/parse-smoke.mjs)),
which parses all 270 files under `client/` and fails if any one of them is not valid JavaScript.
There is no build step, so before this nothing between an editor and a player's browser ever parsed
them, and the existing smokes only import the specific modules they exercise. **The failure it exists
for is the backtick.** Client panels are big HTML template literals, the house style writes long prose
comments inside them, and the house style quotes verbs in `backticks` — so a comment ends the string
mid-sentence. A `` `horn` `` in a `<!-- -->` note in `cab-view.js` killed the entire client boot on
prod (`main.js` reaches every panel transitively: chrome rendered, then no room, no vitals, no socket).
**Inside a template literal, quote identifiers with 'single quotes', never backticks.** One note on the
check itself: it feeds each file to `node --check` on **stdin with `--input-type=module`**, because
plain `node --check <path>` parses a `.js` in the CommonJS goal and *passes* that exact broken file.
Don't "simplify" it to a path argument. Takes ~2 s, no browser, DB or network.

`pretest:regress` also runs **`bigscreen:smoke`** ([scripts/client/bigscreen-smoke.mjs](scripts/client/bigscreen-smoke.mjs)),
which holds together **big screen** — `body.bigscreen`, the rung above each seat's ⛶: the whole
window, no header, no sidebar, no log, no command box, and none of the readouts a seat hangs on its
own glass. It is assembled in four places and every one of them fails silently. A seat with no
stripping rule looks like the mode not working; **a seat that does not call `exitBigScreen()` on its
close path strands the player** — the mode owns the PAGE, so nothing else takes it down, and
climbing out of the cab in big screen leaves a room description with no sidebar, no log and no
command box while the one key that would fix it is bound only while the mode thinks it is on.
⚠ **The seat list is DERIVED** from who calls `windshieldHTML(`, so a fifth seat arrives as a
failure rather than as a gap; `OWNER` says where each seat's chrome lives (helm-view paints,
helm-mode owns the buttons and the close) and an entry is a REASON, never a name. ⚠ **And the seat
scan reads RAW source where every other check here blanks comments first**, which cost a run that
found one seat out of four: `blank()` wipes a template literal WHOLE, interpolations included —
whatever its header's *"a template interpolation is just code again"* reads like — and three of the
four seats build their markup as a template, so `${windshieldHTML(id, label)}` is blanked and the
seat disappears. Mutation-tested 23 of 23. The layout half is unverifiable headlessly (whether a
CSS rule wins is a browser's question), so what it checks there is that each page element is
NAMED; a rect census in a scratch page is where that was measured — serve `client/` as the web root
(not the repo root: modules resolve `/shared/…` absolutely), open each seat into `#area-content`
with no server and no login, and list every visible descendant that is neither inside a `.ws-wrap`
nor an ancestor of one. The empty list is the proof, and it went 238 → 1 for the cab (the clipped
screen-reader record), 140 → 0, 54 → 0 and 6 → 0 for the other three.

⚠ **AND THE LADDER IS TWO AXES, NOT FOUR RUNGS — WHICH IS WHY FREE LOOK HAD ONLY ONE OF THEM.**
⊟ and ⛶ fold the COLUMN (the log, then the command box, both stacked under the pane) and have never
touched the 240px of sidebar beside it, so a seat at fullscreen is still looking through a picture a
fifth narrower than the window. `body.nosidebar` is that other axis and the two COMPOSE — measured,
a free-look pane goes 1007 → 1039 px wide at ⛶ and **1039 → 1280** with the sidebar off, with the
log still up if you want it. ⚠ **It is cleared when the seat closes, like big screen and for a worse
reason**: there is no sidebar switch anywhere in the base UI, so a player who shut a seat with it on
would be left looking at a game missing a fifth of its interface with nothing to say why. ⚠ **AND
FREE LOOK SHIPPED WITH NO ⊟/⛶ AT ALL** on a stated reason that was about the wrong noun — *"the pane
was already all picture"* is true of the pane's CONTENTS (there is no cockpit out here to make room
for) and says nothing about its SIZE, and `#area-pane` is capped at `min(65%, --pane-cap)` of the
column. The ladder is the PAGE giving the pane more room, never the seat giving the picture more
room inside the pane, and free look wants it for exactly the reason the other four do.

⚠ **AND THE CAMERA CAN PUT ITS FEET DOWN FROM A BUTTON NOW (`freeCam.setStand`).** `freecam.js` has
had a standing mode since the telescopes shipped and **nothing could reach it without being a
vantage** — the only way in was `open({stand: true})`, which is the wrong verb for a toggle because
it resets the yaw, the pitch, the lens and the speed ladder on purpose (the shot a panel hands over
at open time is the only one it can honestly describe). Re-opening to change one constraint throws
away the frame the player pressed the button to keep. `setStand` changes the constraints in place:
measured, yaw 181 and pitch 0.228 survive and z drops 1.4 → 0.12 (`STAND_EYE`). ⚠ **Constraints
before position**, `open`'s own rule and for its own reason — `place` reads `eye` and `leash`, so
setting them after it spends a frame at an unleashed height and the shot drops into itself. ⚠ **And
the anchor is where you are STANDING, not where you were put down**, or a camera that flew half the
basin and then stood up is snapped back to the tile it launched from. ⚠ **THE LEASH IS 0, WHICH IS
THE WHOLE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN A PERSON AND A TELESCOPE**: a vantage takes `STAND_LEASH` because it IS
a place, and this is somebody on foot — measured, it walks 4.4 tiles off its anchor and stays at eye
height. ⚠ **`freeCam.standing` IS NOT `st.stand`**: the second is "this view was OPENED as a
vantage", which decides the label, the recentre branch, the detail tune and whether the mount
re-asserts the eye height every frame; the first is "the feet are down right now". Reading one for
the other puts a telescope's mount under a walking camera. ⚠ **A vantage gets no FPS button at all**
— it is already standing, on a mount, and the whole of what a telescope is is that you cannot walk
off with it. ⚠ **And the hint follows the MODE rather than the open**: the standing line is a
different sentence rather than the flying one with clauses struck out, so a camera advertising the
orbit, the roll and the up-down after its feet are down is advertising three controls that refuse.

⚠ **AND THE GATE'S FIRST CUT OF THE RUNG RULE INVENTED FOUR FINDINGS, WHICH IS THE LESSON.** Asking
that every exported `close*` clear every `X-fullscreen`/`X-hidepanel` its own file toggles is the
obvious generalisation and is wrong for a file with two seats in it: **cockpit.js holds the flight
sim and the charter cabin**, so it made `closeCockpit` answer for the flight sim's layout and
reported four bugs that are not bugs. Which close owns which seat is not something the text can say.
What IS checkable is that a rung the file can turn ON is one *some* close path takes back down — so
the two PAGE-level classes (big screen, the sidebar) are still asked of every close, because either
seat can have set them, and the per-seat rungs are asked of the file. Mutation-tested 7 of 7, with
the cab's own rung as the control that the rule reaches further than the seat that prompted it.

`pretest:regress` also runs **`imports:smoke`** ([scripts/imports/smoke.mjs](scripts/imports/smoke.mjs)),
which checks that every **named** import across `server/`, `plugins/`, `client/`, `scripts/`, `tests/`
and `tools/` resolves to something the target module actually exports. ESM resolves these at LINK
time, so a missing export is not a bug you hit when the feature is used — the module never loads and
everything it owns silently stops existing.

⚠ **It has two modes, and the one that matters reads a COMMIT.** Bare, it checks the working tree.
With `--ref <oid>` it checks out a detached worktree and reads that instead, and **the pre-push hook
runs it that way against every oid being pushed** — because the working tree is not what you are
pushing. That distinction is the whole reason it exists: on 2026-09-01 a commit shipped
